CyberTrain had string, text, integer, float, boolean, datetime and binary (D40), stored datetimes as `2026-10-05T09:00:00Z`, and kept a date as a String. [The phase 2 design](superpowers/specs/2026-11-06-phase2-data-layer-design.md) section 2 has the full specification.
- Decision, `decimal`: `t.decimal :price, precision: 18, scale: 8` writes `DECIMAL(18,8)`; the attribute is a `Float`, rounded with `Float#round(scale)` (non-finite values passed through) wherever a value is stored: `to_row`, `update_columns`, `update_all`, the bulk writes. `precision` is DDL only. Revisit when Spinel's `bigdecimal` can be built from a Float or String, round, and compare, and has been shown to compile inside a framework program.
- Decision, `json`: `t.json` writes `JSON`; the attribute is `Hash<String, Object>` (`Cast.json_hash` for NOT NULL, `{}` for a cell that is not a JSON object; `Cast.hash_or_nil` for a nullable one), written with `JSON.generate`. Nested values stay boxed (D22), narrowed with `Cast.int` / `hash` / `array`. A root Array is not supported. Assigned text that does not parse becomes `{}`.
- Decision, datetime: stored as Rails stores it, `YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM:SS.ffffff` in UTC (`Cast.sql_datetime`), by every writer (`Connection#bind`, `Model.now_string`, the job, lease and stream tables, `Cast.to_sql`). The C reader returns microseconds since the epoch and accepts `T` or a space, 0 to 9 fraction digits and `Z` or `±HH:MM` / `±HHMM`; `Cast.parse_time` adds RFC 2822. The display form is a separate method, `Cast.iso8601` (`2026-10-05T09:00:00.000Z`, what Rails' `as_json` writes), used by `as_json`, `to_json` and `Cast.str`. A declared type that upcases to a text starting with `DATETIME` (`datetime(6)`) is a datetime, through one `Connection.datetime_decl?`.
- Decision, `db normalize`: an explicit command rewrites 0.4's cells. For every DATETIME column it selects the cells that do not `GLOB` the canonical shape (a `[0-9]` class at every digit; `LIKE`'s `_` would accept a five-digit fraction with a `Z`), parses each with `Cast.parse_time`, and writes `Cast.sql_datetime` back by `_rowid_`, in one transaction; an unreadable cell is left and counted. A `WITHOUT ROWID` table (`PRAGMA table_list`, so SQLite 3.37.0 or newer, refused clearly below that) or one declaring `_rowid_` in any case (`PRAGMA table_xinfo`, generated columns included) is skipped with its pending count, and the command exits 1 while any is pending, so a deploy stops before the new binaries start.
- Decision, `date`: the standard `Date`, NOT NULL through `Cast.date` on read (a corrupt cell is 1970-01-01 rather than a dead row) and `Cast.date_or_nil` on every write, so invalid or blank input stays nil; every NOT NULL date column gets a generated presence validation, and a defaulted one starts with its default. `t.bigint` writes `BIGINT` and dumps back as `t.bigint` (limit 8); `t.datetime precision: 6` is accepted.
- Decision, parsing: `Time.iso8601`, `Time.rfc2822` and `Time.strptime(text, format)` as class methods on `Time` in cybertrain/time_ext.rb, raising `ArgumentError`; `strptime` takes `%Y %m %d %H %M %S %L %z %Z %b %B %a %A %j %s %%`, `%Z` only UTC / GMT / Z.
- Reason: closer to Rails (its stored format means a Rails database needs no conversion, and one column holds one format; 0.4's `T` sorted after every space-separated value, which broke `ORDER BY` and range conditions on mixed columns); an explicit command over a hidden step in `db migrate` keeps the migrator free of a reserved version.
- Spinel: BigDecimal (the `bigdecimal` package has `+ - * /` only, no construction from Float or String, no `round(n)`), JSON values boxed (NOTES rules 62, 71), a nullable ivar assigned only through a cast that can answer nil (rule 7, every new type), `Cast.date` coexisting with the schema DSL's `TableDef#date` (a singleton and an instance method, compiled together as evidence), and class methods added to `Time` from Ruby source (rule 87).
- Rejected: BigDecimal now (above); a `Money` class (every caller converts; Rails hands out a number); normalizing inside `db migrate`; keeping 0.4's format (a Rails database would need converting the other way).
- Consequences: the compatibility changes for 0.4 applications are listed once in the spec's section 16 and the models guide's "Upgrading from 0.4"; the deploy guides gain the `db normalize` step.
- Verification: `test/cast_types.rb`, `test/time_ext.rb`, `test/db_normalize.rb`, `test/schema.rb`, `test/schema_dumper.rb`, `test/gen_models.rb`.

### D43. The query API: `where` forms, `where.not`, `or`, Integer Ranges and `where_between`, class delegations, `scope`, and the small methods (2026-11-07, phase 2)

- Decision, `where`: one method, `where(cond = nil, *binds)`, dispatching with `case`: a Hash as before plus a Range value with Integer ends (`a..b` is `BETWEEN`, `a...b`, `a..` and `..b` the matching comparisons); a String as a SQL fragment with `?` binds, wrapped in parentheses (so `where("a OR b").where(x)` keeps its meaning); no argument answers the relation, so `where.not(hash)` reads as in Rails (`NOT (... AND ...)`, Rails 6.1's semantics). An Array bind in a String condition raises (use the Hash form for `IN`); any other argument type raises. A json or encrypted column as a key raises `ArgumentError`.
- Decision, other bounds: `where_between(column, from, to, exclude_end: false)` for Time, Date, Float and String bounds (nil for an open end), bound through `bind_for`.
- Decision, `or(other)`: `((a) OR (b))`, the binds concatenated, so a later `where` applies to both sides; a side that differs in order, limit, offset or `distinct` raises (Rails' rule).
- Decision, the rest: `order(hash)`, `distinct` (the column readers and `exists?` select DISTINCT; `count` counts a DISTINCT subquery; the aggregates read distinct inputs), `none` (`1 = 0`, answered without a query), `ids`, `update_all` (the save-time casts, decimal rounding included, `updated_at` untouched, the window as an id subselect), `destroy_all`, `find_each(batch:)` (keyset by id, the relation's order ignored), `find_or_create_by`, `find_or_initialize_by`, `find_by!`, `create!`, and the instance `update_columns` (assigns, then one UPDATE of those columns; no callbacks or validations). `select` is not added: a record missing NOT NULL columns would show `0` / `""`, and `MissingAttributeError` has no equivalent in a generated class.
- Decision, class delegations: every Relation query method also exists on the model class as `def self.x(...) = all.x(...)`, emitted from one list in the generator (`DELEGATED`), so `Article.where.not(...)`, `Article.pluck(:id, :title)`, `Article.pluck_int(:id)` and `Article.includes(:comments)` read as in Rails. Left out are `or` and `not` (they take or follow a relation), `page`, `to_sql` and the Array-like `to_a`, `each`, `size`, `empty?`, which Rails' model class does not answer either.
- Decision, `scope`: `scope :recent, -> { order(created_at: :desc) }` and `scope :since, ->(t) { ... }` in `app/models` are read lexically (D38's scanner); the generator copies the parameter list and body into `def since(t) = (...)` on the Relation (the body parenthesized) and `def self.since(t) = all.since(t)` on the class. Nothing is evaluated at generation; a body that is not valid on a Relation fails at Spinel compile time at the generated line. One line only, nothing after the closing brace, no parentheses or commas in a default; a name that is a column, another scope, a Relation method or a reserved Object / Kernel name (`ModelScan::RESERVED_SCOPE_NAMES`, guarded by `script/check-reserved-names`) stops the generator with a one-line message. The scanner ignores a local named `scope` inside a method body.
- Reason: the Rails spellings, typed where they cannot be; `where.not` over a `where_not` name once the spike showed `def not` parses; `where` taking SQL and `order` taking a Hash, because those are what a Rails developer types.
- Spinel: a Range of Time or Date raises `TypeError` when built and a Float or String Range is not matched by `when Range` once boxed (NOTES rule 86, docs/spinel-feedback.md Q), hence Integer ends and `where_between`; a parameter typed `Hash | String` with a splat dispatches by `case` (spike 7); `when Time` before `when Array` (rule 8); no computed `send`, so scopes are copied text (rule 1); one return type per name, so `Model.none` and `PosixTz.none` coexist only because their receivers are distinct and statically resolved (the `Column#limit` precedent).
- Rejected: copy-on-write relations (relations keep mutating `self`; out of this phase); evaluating scope lambdas at run time (no `instance_exec` of a stored block, rule 2); a `where_range` name for the Integer form (Rails writes a Range).
- Consequences: `find_or_create_by` on a narrowed relation creates from the given Hash only (Rails also merges the relation's equality conditions); recorded in the guide. D10's "a scope is an ordinary class method" is superseded.
- Verification: `test/relation_where.rb`, `test/query_extras.rb`, `test/gen_models.rb` (scopes and delegations), `test/model.rb`.

### D44. `pluck` and `pick`: rows of column-typed cells (2026-11-07, phase 2)

- Decision: `pluck(*columns)` with two or more columns answers `Array<Array<Cell>>`, `Cell` the union `Integer | Float | String | true | false | Time | Date | nil` seeded as `Cast.binds` seeds its Array; each cell is cast by the column's type from the generated Relation's `column_type` (no decltype inspection): integer, float and decimal, boolean, datetime (microseconds), date; string, text, json, binary and encrypted columns answer the stored text or bytes. One column raises `ArgumentError` naming `pluck_str` / `pluck_int` / `pluck_float` / `ids`. `pick(*columns)` is the first row with `LIMIT 1`, or nil. Column names only (`checked_column`); `distinct` applies.
- Reason: multi-column `pluck` is how fin_dash builds chart series; Rails' flat-or-nested answer cannot be typed, and `[[1], [2]]` for one column would be a silent trap.
- Spinel: one return type per method name (NOTES rules 10, 34, 41); polymorphic containers must be seeded (rules 9, 46, 71). The template interpreter's private `pick(items, indices)` was renamed `pick_indices` for rule 34.
- Rejected: an untyped `pluck` answering Strings (every caller would parse); a per-call type argument (the return type would still be the union).
- Verification: `test/pluck.rb`.

### D45. Typed aggregates: why not `maximum(:x)` (2026-11-07, phase 2)

- Decision: `minimum_int` / `_float` / `_time`, `maximum_*`, `average` (`Float | nil`); grouped `sum_int_by`, `sum_float_by`, `minimum_*_by`, `maximum_*_by`, `average_by` answering `Hash<String, T>` keyed by the group column's cell through `Cast.str` (NULL and `""` share `""`, as `count_by` since D22), ascending; four expression forms `sum_float_sql`, `minimum_float_sql`, `maximum_float_sql`, `average_sql`; and `having_sql(sql, binds)`, a chain method for the grouped readers. Under `distinct` every aggregate reads distinct inputs (`SUM(DISTINCT col)`, also inside the `_by` forms; `MIN` / `MAX` are the same either way); with a limit or offset the window is aggregated, so a windowed distinct aggregate reads the first n distinct values. A relation with `having_sql` refuses every non-grouped reader, and a grouped reader under a limit or offset refuses it, rather than drop it silently. An aggregate over an expression and a group, or over two tables, is `DB.with`.
- Reason: Rails' `maximum(:views)` answers an Integer for one column and a Time for another, and `group(:x).sum(:y)` a Hash where `sum(:y)` answers a number; the names carry the type instead. `group` is not a flag because a flag cannot change a method's return type.
- Spinel: one return type per method name (NOTES rules 10, 34, 41). `grouped_cell` returns raw rows and the typed collectors cast them, since a cast argument would mix return types.
- Rejected: a polymorphic `Cell` result (every caller narrows); more `_sql` forms (four cover the survey's uses).
- Verification: `test/aggregates.rb`.

### D46. Bulk writes: `insert_all`, `insert_all!`, `upsert_all` (2026-11-07, phase 2)

- Decision: class methods on the generated model (they need `COLUMN_TYPES`, `DECIMAL_SCALES`, `ENCRYPTED_COLUMNS`), bodies in `Model.insert_all_into`. Multi-row `INSERT ... VALUES (...), (...)` with `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING` (`insert_all`), none (`insert_all!`, a duplicate raises) or `ON CONFLICT (unique_by) DO UPDATE SET c = excluded.c` for the columns the rows supply minus `id`, `unique_by` and `created_at` (`upsert_all`; `update_only:` narrows, and a name the rows do not supply is skipped). `RETURNING` the asked columns (`[:id]` by default) cast by type, as `Array<Hash<String, Cell>>`. Every row has the same key set, and an unknown column raises, before anything is written; values take the save-time casts; timestamps get one `now` per call unless `record_timestamps: false`. Rows per statement are `sqlite3_limit(SQLITE_LIMIT_VARIABLE_NUMBER) / columns` (a new FFI binding, `Connection#variable_limit`), every chunk in one `DB.transaction` (joining an open one). No callbacks, validations or encryption: an encrypted column as a row key, in `returning:`, `unique_by:` or `update_only:` raises.
- Reason: fin_dash loads market data in batches; Rails' names and options. As in Rails, `DO NOTHING` consumes ids for skipped duplicates.
- Spinel: polymorphic bind Arrays seeded (NOTES rule 9); `RETURNING` needs SQLite 3.35.0 or newer; CyberTrain links the system libsqlite3, and the one spike 6 ran against is 3.45.1 with a variable limit of 250000, which the code still asks for at run time (an older library refuses the statement with its own syntax error; `db normalize`, which needs 3.37.0, checks the version first and refuses with a clear message).
- Rejected: a single statement whatever the size (another build's limit may be 999); building records and saving each (the point is to skip that).
- Verification: `test/bulk_writes.rb`.

### D47. Declared associations, `has_many` as a Relation, `dependent:`, counter caches, and `includes` (2026-11-07, phase 2)

- Decision, declarations: `has_many`, `has_one` and `belongs_to` lines in `app/models/*.rb` with literal options (`class_name:`, `foreign_key:`, `dependent:`, `optional:`, `counter_cache:`) are scanned lexically; a missing model, column or option, a trailing comment, or a non-literal fails generation with the file and line. Foreign keys without a declaration keep giving associations (D10); `has_one` on a key replaces its `has_many`. The planning and emission live in generator/association_emitter.rb, moved out of models_emitter.rb.
- Decision, readers: every `has_many` (declared or from a foreign key) returns the child's generated Relation (`CommentRelation.new("comments").where(article_id: @id)`); `read_association` returns its `to_a`, so templates are unchanged. Every `belongs_to` caches with three ivars: `@x_loaded` (a Boolean, since any Integer is a valid explicit id), `@x_loaded_key` (the key it was read for) and `@x_record` (nil for a missing parent), used only while the key is unchanged, so a reassigned key never answers the stale parent; `has_one` caches with `@x_loaded` and `@x_record` and answers the first child by id. `reload` clears every cache and preload.
- Decision, validation: a declared `belongs_to` without `optional: true` emits `validates_foreign_key :x_id` ("can't be blank" while the key is nil, blank or 0), not `presence`, because a NOT NULL integer key starts at 0.
- Decision, `dependent:`: `:destroy` (each child's `destroy`, callbacks and its own `dependent:`), `:delete_all` (one DELETE), `:nullify` (one UPDATE; the key must be nullable) inside `destroy`, before the parent's DELETE, in one `DB.transaction` joining an open one. `on_delete: :cascade | :nullify | :restrict` is the schema side (D50); the guide says which to pick (callbacks need the former; speed and other writers favour the latter).
- Decision, `counter_cache: true` (on `belongs_to` only; refused on `has_many`): `after_create` increments and `after_destroy` decrements `<children>_count` (the key read from the row inside destroy's transaction); a moved child is counted on both sides by a generated `before_update` that reads the persisted key from the database into `@x_id_was` for that save only, inside a transaction the save opens when the model is counter-cached; the parent's own save leaves the counter out of its UPDATE (its copy may be stale). A parent destroyed with `dependent: :destroy` decrements itself once per child (harmless).
- Decision, `includes(*names)` (and `preload`): `to_a` runs one query per name (`Child.where(fk: ids).order(id: :asc)` for `has_many` / `has_one`, `Parent.where(id: keys)` for `belongs_to`), the ids chunked at the variable limit, and fills every record's slot, nil or empty where nothing matched. A preloaded `has_many` Relation answers `to_a` (a copy), `each`, `first`, `last`, `size`, `empty?` from memory; `count` and `exists?` query; a chain method drops the preload. One level; a preloaded child does not get its inverse set.
- Reason: Rails' vocabulary, and a Relation-returning `has_many` so that `article.comments.where(...)`, `.find(id)` and `includes` chain; N+1 in index pages was the survey's most common performance item.
- Spinel: no custom `x_id=` writer on a generated model (NOTES rule 43), hence the key comparison instead of a writer that clears the cache; a stored callback block called with a base-class receiver runs the base stub (rule 88, new), so the generated counter-cache hooks have no stub on `Model`; literal option text only (rule 1).
- Rejected: polymorphic associations (the reader's return type would be a union of model classes), `has_many :through`, scoped `has_many`, `inverse_of`, `dependent: :restrict_with_error`, nested `includes`, `eager_load`, `joins`.
- Consequences: compatibility change 1 of the spec's section 16 (`.to_a` before Array-only methods), which also covers foreign-key-derived readers.
- Verification: `test/associations.rb`, `test/includes.rb`, `test/gen_models.rb`, `test/gen_models_compiles.rb`, examples/blog.

### D48. Validators and the generated validation `case`s (2026-11-07, phase 2)

- Decision: `validates(*args)` (the attributes, then one options Hash) gains `inclusion` / `exclusion` (an Array or an Integer Range), `format` (`with:` / `without:`), `numericality` (a String parsed first; a Float fails `only_integer`, as in Rails), `uniqueness` (`true` or `{ scope:, case_sensitive: }`), the fuller `length` (`minimum`, `maximum`, `is`, `in`), and on every rule `allow_nil:`, `allow_blank:` (now also for `presence` on the same line, a compatibility change), `on:` (one Symbol), `message:` (also inside a rule's Hash). The rules stay run-time `Validator` objects in `VALIDATORS[model]`; an unknown option raises `ArgumentError` when the model loads. Messages are Rails' English defaults, without its singular form ("1 characters").
- Decision, uniqueness: the generated `unique_value_taken?(attr, value, scope_attr, scope_value, exclude_id, case_sensitive)` (six arguments, as the plan had it, so that `case_sensitive` reaches the query) builds `where(...)` / `LOWER(col) = LOWER(?)`, `IS NULL` for nil, `where.not(id:)`, `exists?`; a `Model` stub keeps the base class compiling. It is refused on an encrypted column at generation, with a run-time raise as a belt.
- Decision, `validate :method` and conditions: `validate :a, :b, on:, if:, unless:` lines are scanned and emitted as `run_custom_validations(context)`, a sequence of literal calls; `if:` / `unless:` on either take Symbol method names, emitted as `validation_condition?(name)`, a `case` over the names seen with `else raise ArgumentError`. A lambda, the `:if =>` and `"if":` spellings, and a name that is neither a `def` in the model file, a column nor an association stop the generator.
- Reason: Rails' validators are the most-used model API in the survey; Rails' messages so that tests and views port as they are.
- Spinel: no computed `send` (NOTES rule 1), hence the generated `case`s; `self.class` only through generated per-model methods (rules 3, 4), hence `unique_value_taken?`; only Integer Ranges (rule 86).
- Rejected: generating a method per rule (the run-time objects already work and keep `gen/` small); i18n of messages (phase 4); `validates_with`, `validates_each`, `confirmation`, `acceptance`, `valid?(context)`.
- Verification: `test/validators.rb`, `test/gen_models.rb`.

### D49. Encryption: `encrypts`, `Cybertrain::Encryptor` (AES-256-GCM), and the keys (2026-11-07, phase 2)

- Decision: `Cybertrain::Encryptor` in cybertrain/encryptor.rb, AES-256-GCM through `OpenSSL::Cipher`, a fresh 12-byte nonce per call, the payload `"1:" + Base64(nonce ‖ ciphertext ‖ tag)`; a wrong key, a changed byte, an unknown version, bad Base64 or a short payload raises `Encryptor::Error`; `decrypt("")` answers `""` so a NOT NULL column added with `DEFAULT ''` stays readable. `Crypto.encrypt` / `decrypt` (and `_or_nil`) use `Config#encryptor`, built once per key text. `encrypts :api_key` in a model is scanned: the generated `load_row` decrypts and `to_row` encrypts; the column is listed in `ENCRYPTED_COLUMNS` and the Relation refuses it in `where`, `where_like`, `where_between`, `update_all`, `increment`, `upsert` (the generated one passes `ENCRYPTED_COLUMNS`), the bulk writes and `uniqueness`; `pluck` answers the payload; `to_json` includes the text (as Rails).
- Decision, keys: `CYBERTRAIN_ENCRYPTION_KEY` (64 hex characters, stripped) and `CYBERTRAIN_ENCRYPTION_KEY_PREVIOUS` (comma-separated, decryption only), read as Strings into `Config#encryption_key` / `encryption_keys_previous`; not derived from `secret_key_base`, so rotating the session secret leaves data readable. Boot runs the String check `Config#encryption_error` always: a malformed key or previous entry fails boot, and an empty key fails it when gen/app.rb has set `Config.encryption_required` (a model declares `encrypts`). `cybertrain secret --hex` prints a key; `cybertrain new` writes the variable, commented, into `.env.example`.
- Decision, opt-in openssl: `require "cybertrain"` does not load the encryptor; gen/app.rb requires `cybertrain/encryptor` when a model declares `encrypts`, and an application that calls `Crypto.encrypt` by hand adds that require itself. This differs from the spec, which assumed the framework already linked openssl: only `cybertrain/net/tls` does, macOS needs Homebrew's OpenSSL for it, and a program with openssl dispatches `getbyte` on a boxed String through the package's own method (NOTES rule 62), so a program without encrypted columns should not pay for it.
- Reason: fin_dash stores API keys with `encrypts`; Rails' macro name; authenticated encryption is what a column wants, and Spinel's openssl offers aes-128-gcm and aes-256-gcm.
- Spinel: keys and nonces must be raw bytes (`Crypto.random_token` answers hex, so `Encryptor.from_hex` packs the configured text); `Crypto.encrypt` shares a name with `OpenSSL::Cipher#encrypt` and compiles because the receivers are distinct and static (the `Column#limit` precedent).
- Rejected: `deterministic: true` (a searchable encryption needs a second key and a fixed nonce scheme, out of this phase); reading Rails' `ActiveRecord::Encryption` envelope (its key derivation differs; the guide shows the re-encrypt loop); deriving the key from `secret_key_base`.
- Verification: `test/encryptor.rb`, `test/boot_encryption.rb` (a child process boots with and without the key), `test/openssl_dispatch.rb`, `test/gen_models.rb`.

### D50. Schema and migration additions, the table rebuild, and taking over a Rails database (2026-11-07, phase 2)

- Decision, schema DSL and dumper: `t.decimal` (precision, scale; a scale without a precision is an `ArgumentError`), `t.json`, `t.bigint`, `t.date`, `t.datetime precision:`; `on_delete:` on `t.references`, `add_reference` and `add_foreign_key` (`ON DELETE CASCADE | SET NULL | RESTRICT`; `:nullify` refused on a NOT NULL reference), read back from `PRAGMA foreign_key_list`; `t.references index: false`; partial indexes (`where:`) in migrations and the schema, expression indexes (a String column spec, which needs `name:`) in the schema DSL, both recovered by the dumper from `sqlite_master.sql` (`PRAGMA index_info` names an expression NULL); expression defaults such as `(datetime('now'))` keep their parentheses. The dumper skips `ar_internal_metadata` and maps Rails' type names (`datetime(6)`, `decimal(18,8)`, `varchar`, `bigint`, `json`, `numeric`).
- Decision, rebuilding migrations: `change_column`, `change_column_default` (`to:` required; `from:` makes it reversible), `change_column_null` (the fourth argument backfills NULL cells and never becomes a DEFAULT; rolling back `null: true` needs it), `remove_reference` and `remove_foreign_key` go through one `SqliteDDL.rebuild_table` following SQLite's twelve-step procedure: dump the live table, create `<table>_new` from the changed definition, copy the common columns, drop, rename (inside `PRAGMA legacy_alter_table = ON`, so views and triggers on other tables that name it survive, reset in `ensure`), recreate its indexes and triggers from `sqlite_master.sql`, and restore the `sqlite_sequence` high-water mark. The migrator turns `PRAGMA foreign_keys` off before `BEGIN` for a version that rebuilds (it is a no-op inside a transaction, and left on, dropping the old table would cascade), runs `PRAGMA foreign_key_check` before `COMMIT` (a violation, anywhere in the database, rolls the version back as `DB::Error` naming the rows) and turns it back on. `rename_table` is a plain `ALTER TABLE ... RENAME TO`, reversible, keeping index names. `reversible { |dir| dir.up { execute } dir.down { execute } }` records `:reversible_up` / `:reversible_down` operations, `execute` only, each block's statements in written order on its own direction. `Operation` keeps its one-class shape with new kinds and two fields (`from_default`, `from_given`).
- Decision, refusing what a rebuild would drop: before rebuilding, a token scan of the table's `CREATE TABLE` text refuses CHECK, inline UNIQUE, COLLATE, generated columns, ON UPDATE, ON DELETE SET DEFAULT, DEFERRABLE, an ON CONFLICT clause, a foreign key's MATCH, a foreign key to a column other than `id`, an untyped column, a primary key other than `id`, a non-integer primary key on `id` (Rails' `id: :uuid` / `id: :string`), WITHOUT ROWID and STRICT ("rebuilding <t> would drop <what>: rewrite the migration with execute"); a plain `id INTEGER PRIMARY KEY` gains AUTOINCREMENT, and a table without id rebuilds without copying one. It is a scan, not a parser: those words count only outside quotes and comments, so an unquoted column named `match` or `conflict` is refused too (loudly; Rails quotes every identifier).
- Decision, takeover: `db schema` writes `db/schema.rb` from the live database with the migrate dumper and prints its source; the migrator ignores an applied version with no file (neither run nor rolled back; `db status` shows `applied (no file)`), so Rails' `schema_migrations` rows need no cleanup. A table whose primary key is not an integer `id` column (a Rails join table, or Rails' `id: :uuid` / `id: :string`, its varchar `id` kept as a plain column) is dumped as `create_table "x", id: false` with every column; `SqliteDDL` writes no implicit id for it and the generator makes no model of it (nor a `has_many` pointing at it), printing `skipped x: no id column`. The models guide's "Taking over a database Rails created" has the five steps.
- Reason: Rails' migration vocabulary (survey D-5, D-6, D-8), and data safety over convenience: a rebuild that silently lost a constraint would be worse than a refusal that sends the author to `execute`.
- Spinel: none beyond the framework's usual (the rebuild is SQL); the refusal list exists because the schema model, not Spinel, is the limit.
- Rejected: `ALTER TABLE` emulation without a rebuild (SQLite has none for a column); renaming indexes with the table (Rails does; kept as is, recorded); an expression index helper in migrations (`execute` writes it, and the dump keeps it).
- Consequences: a hand-written table with constraints the schema does not model is changed with `execute`; a pre-existing foreign key violation anywhere blocks a rebuilding version, as in Rails.
- Verification: `test/migration_rebuild.rb`, `test/migration.rb`, `test/migrator.rb`, `test/schema_dumper.rb`, `test/schema_dumper_rails.rb` (the DDL Rails 8.1 writes), `test/db_cli.rb`.

### D51. Job classes, retry policies, concurrency keys and the `Run`-taking registration (2026-11-09, phase 3)

- Context: fin_dash's jobs are Active Job classes with `retry_on`, `discard_on` and Solid Queue's `limits_concurrency ... key:`; D28 had a block per String kind with one retry policy for all. [The phase 3 design](superpowers/specs/2026-11-07-phase3-jobs-outbound-design.md) sections 2 to 4 and 11 have the full specification, and its section 18 what differed.
- Decision, classes: `class WelcomeJob < Cybertrain::Job::Base` in `app/jobs/` (nested directories too) with `def perform(args)`, `args` the `Hash<String, Object>` read back from the JSON payload (one Hash: positional or keyword arguments cannot be typed across job classes). `Gen::JobScan` reads the files as text (D38's method); `gen/jobs.rb`, written on every generator run, reopens each class with `job_kind`, `perform_later` / `perform_at` / `perform_in` (the row id), `perform_now` (runs `perform` on the calling thread with no rescue, `nil`) and `run_job(run)`, and registers it with `Job.register("WelcomeJob", concurrency:, concurrency_key:, max_attempts:) { |run| WelcomeJob.run_job(run) }`. The kind is the class name, so `Job.handle` kinds and classes share one namespace. `Job::Base` holds only `perform` (raises `NotImplementedError`), `retries_exhausted(error)` and the macro no-ops. `ApplicationJob` is scanned but not registered: its arms follow each subclass's own, its `limits_concurrency` applies to one that sets none; any other job class can be a parent and is a job of its own. `cybertrain generate job NAME [--retry Klass:wait:attempts]` writes the class and a test.
- Decision, policies: `retry_on Klass, wait: n | :polynomially_longer, attempts: n` (wait 3 and attempts 5 when left out, Rails' defaults; `attempts ** 4 + 2` seconds for the polynomial) and `discard_on Klass` become literal `rescue` arms of `run_job` in declaration order, the first match winning; an unmatched exception keeps D28's default policy. `Job.run_handler` gives that policy to `NotImplementedError`, `SystemStackError` and `ScriptError` too (not `StandardError`s, but what a job's own code raises), and `claim_safely` logs any other `Exception` and keeps the job thread alive, leaving the row `running` for heartbeat recovery. `Job.retry_later(run, error, wait, polynomial, attempts)` and `Job.discard(run, error)` write the outcome with phase 1's fence and answer `OUTCOME_REQUEUED` / `OUTCOME_FAILED` / `OUTCOME_STALE`; the generated arm runs `retries_exhausted` only on `OUTCOME_FAILED`, never for an attempt another worker reclaimed. The state `discarded` joins the four of D28 (`state_count`, `Job.prune_finished` for `failed` and `discarded`; `prune_done` unchanged). Stale-heartbeat recovery fails a row only at `Job.recovery_limit(kind)`, the larger of `Config#job_max_attempts` and the kind's largest `attempts:`.
- Decision, keys: `limits_concurrency to: n, key: "field"` stores `Job.concurrency_key_for(kind, payload)` (`Cast.str` of the parsed payload's field) in the new column `cybertrain_jobs.concurrency_key` at enqueue (a missing field raises `ArgumentError`), and the claim excludes each (kind, key) pair at its limit. One derivation serves the enqueue, the backfill `Job.start!` runs over `queued` rows enqueued before the kind was keyed (batches of 1000, idempotent, unkeyable ids listed at warn), and the keyed recovery of stale `running` rows (one UPDATE per row that sets the key with the state). `install!` adds the column and an index on `(state, kind, concurrency_key)` in place.
- Decision, test helpers (`cybertrain/test/jobs`): `assert_enqueued_jobs`, `assert_enqueued_with` (a `Cast.str` comparison per key, a Symbol key read as its name), `assert_no_enqueued_jobs`, `perform_enqueued_jobs(now)` (`Job.work_one` until none is due; it fails the test after 10,000 jobs, a job re-enqueuing itself due at once) and `clear_enqueued_jobs`, top-level like the other assertions; the support file `cybertrain generate authentication` writes requires them.
- Reason: closer to Rails (the class, the macros and the test helpers are what a Rails developer types), and a policy per exception class is what fin_dash's API jobs need. The concurrency key is Solid Queue's, without its `duration:` (a stale heartbeat frees the slot, the same guarantee).
- Spinel: no `constantize` or computed `send` (NOTES rule 1), so the registry is generated code and `cron`'s `job:` is a String kind; no stored block run through a base receiver (rule 88), so the policy is literal arms in a class method and `retries_exhausted` an instance method called on a concrete object; one return type per name (10 / 34 / 41), hence `Run`'s prefixed readers (`run_id`, `run_args`, ...); and rule 89, found in this phase's spike: a `rescue` arm naming a qualified class the program does not define can match nothing or swallow everything, so the generator accepts only core classes, the framework's `...Error` classes (adding their require to `gen/app.rb`), and application classes defined in `app/models/`, `app/jobs/application_job.rb` or above the macro in the job's file, and refuses the rest with a one-line message.
- Rejected: a generic `perform_later` on the base class (it would need the subclass's name at run time: a `case` table anyway); a `retry_on ... do |job, error|` block (a stored block, rule 88); positional job arguments; `job:` as a class constant (a class in a variable cannot dispatch a class method).
- Consequences: an application that requires `cybertrain` always has a registered kind (the mail job of D57), so every process that runs jobs starts its job threads; the worker banner's "no job handler" branch is unreachable for such a program and kept for a program that loads the job file alone. A keyed kind added to a running deployment needs D39's order (stop every old process first). `jobs` is a built-in word of `Main` (D53).
- Verification: `test/jobs.rb` (policies, fence, `discarded`, keys, backfill, keyed recovery, `recovery_limit`), `test/job_threads.rb`, `test/gen_jobs.rb`, `test/gen_jobs_compiles.rb` (the fixture's `WelcomeJob` and `Admin::ReportJob` compiled and run), `test/cli_generate_job.rb`, `test/gen_manifest.rb`, `test/job_helpers.rb`, examples/blog's `NotifyCommentJob`.

### D52. Cron schedules and the persisted timetable (2026-11-09, phase 3)

- Decision: `Scheduler.cron(expr, name, tz: "", catch_up: false, job: "", args: {}) { }` beside `every`. `Cybertrain::Cron` parses five fields (`*`, `*/n`, `a-b`, `a,b`, `a-b/n`, month and weekday names, weekday 0 and 7 Sunday; no `@hourly`) and `Cron.next_after(expr, epoch, zone)` is a pure function over D19's `TimeZone`: the first wall-clock minute after the task's last run that matches in the zone, a skipped wall time not run, and in a fold Vixie cron's rule (a wildcard-hour expression at both occurrences, a fixed-hour one once). `tz: ""` reads `Config#scheduler_tz` (`CYBERTRAIN_SCHEDULER_TZ`, `"UTC"`) when the scheduler starts. A task takes a block (run on the scheduler thread) or `job:`, a kind it only enqueues, checked against the registry at boot. A bad expression raises `ArgumentError` naming the task when `config/app.rb` loads.
- Decision, the timetable: `cybertrain_schedules (name, last_run_at, next_run_at, updated_at)`, created on first use and out of `db/schema.rb`. The lease holder (D39) reads it at start and on every takeover, writes `next_run_at` when it schedules and `last_run_at` when it runs (never moving a row backwards), and `Scheduler.prune_schedules` removes names no longer registered at start. An `every` task overdue across a restart runs once at start; a missed `cron` slot is skipped (cron's and Solid Queue's rule) unless `catch_up: true`; on a takeover a slot that came due while this process was running but no leader ran it runs once, whatever `catch_up` says. A task whose row has no run yet takes the earlier of the stored `next_run_at` and the next run its current schedule gives from now, so a schedule shortened by a release before the first run is not held to the old slot. An expression with no match within five years from a run (a 29 February late in the century) is logged once and the task is disabled until restart.
- Reason: fin_dash's recurring work is time-of-day work in Asia/Tokyo; persisting the timetable stops a restart or a leader change from resetting an interval or running every overdue task at once (the phase 1 judgement call this replaces).
- Spinel: none beyond D28's (a task's block is a stored Proc called from a plain method, rules 32 / 45); the parser and `next_after` run under CRuby and compiled alike.
- Rejected: `@hourly`-style shortcuts; replaying every missed slot; the spike's "a wall minute runs once" fold rule (a two-hour gap in real time for an hourly entry).
- Consequences: a task row survives a rename until the next start; delivery of a task around a change of leader is at least once, as for jobs.
- Verification: `test/cron.rb` (Europe/London and Asia/Tokyo transitions, the fold rule), `test/scheduler_cron.rb` (a restart on one database, leader changes and takeovers, `catch_up`, `job:`, the uncomputable case, `scheduler_tz`), `test/scheduler.rb`.

### D53. The jobs page and the `jobs` command (2026-11-09, phase 3)

- Decision: `jobs_page "/admin/jobs", before_action: :require_admin` in `config/routes.rb` records a `Gen::JobsPageSpec` and six unnamed routes; the generator emits `CybertrainJobsController < ApplicationController` into `gen/controllers.rb` with that `before_action` and the actions `index`, `retry`, `discard`, `delete`, `retry_all` and `discard_all_failed` (methods named `<action>_action`: `retry` is a keyword). `Cybertrain::JobsPage` builds the HTML with the tag builder (D38), renders it inside the application's layout, and its buttons are `button_to` forms with the CSRF token. The page shows the counts per state, the queued count and next due time, the `failed` and `discarded` rows and the `running` ones, `Config#jobs_page_per_page` (50) rows per list with `?page=N`. Every row action (`JobsPage.retry_job` / `discard_job` / `delete_job`) is `WHERE id = ? AND state IN ('failed', 'discarded')`; a retry resets `attempts` and writes the row's derived concurrency key in the same UPDATE. Without `before_action:` the page is open and `spin run gen` prints a warning. `./<app> jobs list [STATE] | retry ID | retry --all | discard ID | delete ID | prune [--before DAYS]` (`Job::CLI`, prune default 7 days, `Job.prune_finished`; `list` prints the newest 1000 rows and says how many there are when there are more) shares the row actions and starts no thread.
- Reason: Mission Control's page, inside the application's own authentication rather than a second one; a page and a command because a deployment without a browser session still needs to retry a failed job.
- Spinel: one return type per name, hence `retry_job` and the other prefixed names on `JobsPage` (rules 10 / 34); no template file ships with the framework (nothing is written into `app/views`).
- Rejected: a page mounted open by default; a framework login; a template the application would have to copy.
- Consequences: the page's path must stay out of `csrf_exempt_prefixes`, or its forms lose CSRF protection (the guide says so); an application without `app/controllers/application_controller.rb` cannot draw one (every `cybertrain new` app has it). examples/blog gates its page with `BLOG_ADMIN_TOKEN` (an `x-admin-token` header, or a session holding an HMAC of the token set at `/admin/login`; 404 when unset).
- Verification: `test/jobs_page.rb`, `test/main_jobs.rb`, `test/gen_routes.rb`, `test/gen_controllers.rb`, `test/gen_runner_models.rb`, examples/blog's admin tests.

### D54. The cache store (2026-11-09, phase 3)

- Decision: `Cybertrain::Cache` (`require "cybertrain/cache"`, not part of `require "cybertrain"`) over `cybertrain_cache (key, value, expires_at, updated_at)` in the application database, created on first use and out of `db/schema.rb`. `fetch(key, expires_in: 0, race_condition_ttl: 0) { String }`, `fetch_hash` / `fetch_array` / `fetch_int` (JSON or Integer text, parsed with `Cast` and `Params.parse_int`, so a corrupt cell reads as `{}` / `[]` / `0`), `read`, `write(..., unless_exist:)` (false only when a live row was kept), `delete`, `exist?`, `increment` (one `INSERT ... ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE ... RETURNING`; an expired row restarts at `by`; `expires_in` sets the expiry only when the counter starts, so its window is fixed) and `clear`. `expires_in: 0` (and a negative value) never expires; a row with `expires_at` E is live through E. `race_condition_ttl: n` extends an expired row by one conditional UPDATE, and only the caller whose UPDATE changed it recomputes. Requiring the file registers the task `"cybertrain cache prune"` every `Config#cache_prune_interval` seconds (300, read at require time), deleting expired rows 1000 per statement; `Cache.prune(now)` is the test entry point.
- Reason: Rails' API names and options, on the database the application already has; String values because Spinel has no `Marshal` and `fetch` must answer one type.
- Spinel: one return type per name (rules 10 / 34 / 41), hence the typed `fetch_*`; rule 90 (new): a `yield` passed straight as a method's argument ran the block twice when blocks of different return types reach it, so every `fetch` assigns the block's value to a local first; `Cache.read` / `write` / `delete` / `clear` keep Rails' spellings beside `BlobStore` and `Session`'s (spike item 6).
- Rejected: an in-memory store (per process, so several workers would disagree); `nil` for "never" (an Integer keyword takes 0); a separate database file and a size cap (out of phase 3).
- Consequences: every cache write takes the SQLite writer lock: the guide recommends caching what jobs produce, or read-mostly values.
- Verification: `test/cache.rb` (expiry, two callers under `race_condition_ttl`, a raising block, `unless_exist`, `increment`, the typed readers, `prune`), examples/blog's cached article count.

### D55. The HTTP client: verbs, streaming, files, redirects, gzip and Server-Sent Events (2026-11-09, phase 3)

- Decision, verbs and JSON: `request(verb, url, body:, content_type:, headers:)` for GET, POST, PUT, PATCH, DELETE and HEAD (a body on GET or HEAD is an `ArgumentError`); `Response#json_hash` / `#json_array` through `Cast`.
- Decision, streaming: with a block, `get` / `post` / `request` yield each body piece (a chunked frame up to 1 MiB, or a read) and return the Response with an empty body; `max_body` does not apply, `timeout` and `deadline` do. `get_to_file(url, path)` writes a 2xx body to `path.part` and renames it onto `path`; any other final status is read and counted but never reaches the disk, and every error removes the `.part`. `Response#bytes_written` counts the body.
- Decision, redirects: `HttpClient.new(redirects: n)` (default 0) follows 301 / 302 / 303 / 307 / 308, resolving a relative `Location`, re-running the private-address check on every hop, refusing https to http (`BlockedAddressError`), turning 303 (and 301 / 302 of a POST) into a body-less GET, dropping `Authorization`, `Cookie` and `Proxy-Authorization` on a cross-origin hop, with one `deadline` for all hops and `TooManyRedirectsError` (host and port only) past the limit.
- Decision, gzip: the support is a substitution point, `HttpClient.gzip=` (the reader is `HttpClient.gzip_decoder`), whose base class lives in `cybertrain/http/gzip_decoder.rb` so that neither the client nor `cybertrain/http/gzip_ffi` pulls the other into a program. `gzip_ffi` installs its decoder when required; `require "cybertrain"` already requires it (it inflates gzip request bodies), so in an application every request sends `Accept-Encoding: gzip` unless the caller sent one. A kept body is limited by `max_body` on the wire and inflated; a streamed one is inflated incrementally, 64 KiB at a time, with no total limit. The Response keeps the received headers (`Content-Encoding`, the wire `Content-Length`). Another coding, a corrupt stream, or gzip without the support loaded is a `BadResponseError`; a zero-byte gzip body is an empty body.
- Decision, Server-Sent Events: `Cybertrain::Sse::Parser` (`cybertrain/sse`), a pure byte-level parser following WHATWG's rules (CR / LF / CRLF across chunk boundaries, a BOM, comments, `id` persisting, `retry` digits only, a blank line without data resetting the event name); `Sse::Event` has `event_name`, `event_data`, `event_id`, `event_retry_ms`; `finish` yields an unterminated last event. No size limit on a line or an event; binary chunks give binary data.
- Reason: fin_dash downloads a 78 MB file, reads AI endpoints over SSE and calls APIs that redirect and compress; each keeps D24's rules (every wait bounded, non-public addresses refused, errors without the path).
- Spinel: one return type per name: the SSE readers are `event_*` (rules 10 / 34 / 41), and the gzip reader is not named `gzip` because Spinel's zlib package defines `Zlib.gzip` answering a String; rule 84 (a call to a class method no class in the program defines does not compile) is why the decoder's base class is in a third file; rescue in non-yielding methods (32 / 45), messages in locals (58); the NOTES 62 grep stays silent with zlib and openssl linked (spike item 4).
- Rejected: keep-alive, proxies, cookies, HTTP/2; a `max_body` for streamed bodies (a stream is bounded in time); renaming a non-2xx `get_to_file` body onto the target.
- Verification: `test/http_client.rb`, `test/http_client_gzip.rb`, `test/sse_parser.rb`, `test/http_gunzip.rb`.

### D56. Feed timers, supervision and the feed health check (2026-11-09, phase 3)

- Decision, timers: `Subscription#on_tick(seconds) { |conn| }` runs on the feed's own thread between its one-second waits, connected or not (`conn` nil while disconnected), first `seconds` after start; an exception is logged and the feed goes on; one per subscription.
- Decision, supervision: `Background.supervise!` starts one supervisor thread per process that runs feeds; once a second it finds feed threads that ended (an exception past their rescues, now logged as `thread ended`, the open connection closed first) and restarts each after `min(30, 2 ** restarts)` seconds with a `[WARN]`. `feed_status` gains `restarts` and `thread_alive?`. In a `feeds` process a feed that dies again after `Config#feed_restart_limit` (10) restarts within `feed_restart_window` (600 s) gives up: `[FATAL] feed NAME restarted N times in W s; exiting`, `Background.feeds_failed?` set, and the role's main thread stops every feed and returns 1, the exit status. A `server` never exits for its feeds. `Background.stop_all` stops the supervisor before the feeds. A non-`StandardError` inside the supervisor's own loop is logged and supervision goes on.
- Decision, health: the supervisor writes one row per feed into `cybertrain_feeds (name, process, connected, started_at, last_activity_at, restarts, updated_at)` every `feed_status_interval` (10 s), through a `WebSocket::StatusWriter` registry that `cybertrain.rb` fills (`FeedTable.upsert_feed`), so the WebSocket files still load without the database. `last_activity_at` is the last message or the last pong. `GET /up` in a server (`Config#health_feeds`, on by default; `CYBERTRAIN_HEALTH_FEEDS=0` turns it off) answers `503 feed stale` when a row's `updated_at`, or independently its `COALESCE(last_activity_at, started_at)`, is older than `health_feed_stale` (180 s; boot refuses a value not above `feed_status_interval` while the check is on); a row whose name no subscription of this binary registers is not counted (every role runs the same binary), and a `feeds` process stopped cleanly deletes the rows it wrote, while one that crashed or gave up leaves them to go stale; a table it cannot read is not stale (200, one WARN). `WebSocket.subscribe` refuses the name `status`. `./<app> feeds status` prints the rows (`FeedTable.read_feeds`). `cybertrain new`'s `config/deploy.yml` gives the `worker` role (and the commented `feeds` one) `restart: unless-stopped`.
- Reason: Kamal polls `/up` only for the proxied role during a deploy and Docker restarts a container only when its process exits, so the supervisor is the first line and the exit the second; publishing through the database lets a server watch feeds that run in other containers.
- Spinel: rule 91 (new): `exit` on a thread other than the main one ends only that thread on this build, so the supervisor signals and the main thread exits; threads spawned through methods (21); `Thread#alive?` and a respawn from another thread work (spike item 3); `Thread#join` re-raises the dead thread's exception, so the joins rescue `Exception` in plain methods (32 / 45); a nullable `conn` (7).
- Rejected: restarting through `/up` (nothing acts on it outside a deploy); exiting a `server` for a feed; a supervisor that exits on its own errors (a database outage would become a crash loop).
- Consequences: status rows are keyed by name only, so two processes running the same feed overwrite each other; a removed or renamed feed's row stays until deleted (ignored by `/up`, marked `(not registered)` by `feeds status`); the restart count never resets, so a feed that has died five times waits 30 s from then on.
- Verification: `test/websocket_subscription.rb` (`on_tick`, the pong refreshing activity), `test/background.rb` (supervision, backoff, give-up, a failing writer), `test/feeds_exit.rb` (a child process exits 1), `test/health.rb`, `test/main_roles.rb`, `test/config_settings.rb`, `test/schema_dumper.rb`.

### D57. HTML mail and `deliver_later` (2026-11-09, phase 3)

- Decision, HTML: `Mailer.deliver(to:, subject:, body:, html: "", from:)`; with `html:` the message is `multipart/alternative` (text part first, then HTML, both `charset=UTF-8` in base64, a boundary from `Crypto.random_token`, the `Content-Type` header folded), built by the pure `Mailer::Message.build` and read back by `Message.parse` (`Test::ReceivedMessage#message_parts`); such a message is sent `7bit`. `Views.render_mail(name, locals)` renders a partial inside `layouts/mail` when it exists.
- Decision, `deliver_later`: `Mailer.deliver_later(...)` checks the fields as `deliver` does and enqueues the built-in kind `"cybertrain mail"` with the finished message as its JSON payload. The job retries `TimeoutError`, `ConnectError` and a `RejectedError` with a 4xx reply 60 s later up to 5 attempts, and discards a `RejectedError` with a 5xx reply and an `ArgumentError` at run time; everything else follows the default policy. It lives in `cybertrain/mailer/later.rb`, which `cybertrain.rb` requires, so `cybertrain/mailer.rb` alone needs no database and loads under CRuby.
- Reason: mail rendered at enqueue time needs no view context in the job and is inspectable on the jobs page; a transient 4xx (greylisting) must not lose mail, a 5xx will not change on retry.
- Spinel: the policy is the generated arms' shape written by hand in a non-yielding method, every class anchored (`::Cybertrain::Mailer::TimeoutError`, rule 89); the multipart text is built with `<<` (rule 60).
- Rejected: parameterised mailer classes (`UserMailer.welcome(user).deliver_later`: a mail is an application method); attachments and inline images; discarding every `RejectedError` (a 4xx is transient).
- Consequences: an `ArgumentError` at run time also covers configuration mistakes (no `smtp_host`, no sender) and a 535 login failure is a 5xx: such mail is discarded until someone reads the jobs page, and `jobs retry ID` re-queues it once the configuration is fixed. A message to several recipients is one transaction, so a 5xx for one discards it for all. Every application registers the kind, so every process that runs jobs starts its job threads.
- Verification: `test/mailer.rb`, `test/mailer_job.rb` (4xx retried, 5xx discarded, `ArgumentError` discarded, a timeout requeued, the fifth attempt failing), `test/views_detached.rb`, spike item 5.

### D58. Routing: `namespace`, `scope`, resource blocks, `controller:`, `param:`, redirect routes and query strings (2026-11-11, phase 4a)

- Decision, the DSL: `Gen::Mapper` gains `scope(path = "", module: "", as: "")` (a path prefix, a controller module, a helper prefix; any of them, combined as in Rails) and `namespace(name)`, which is `scope("/name", module: "name", as: "name")`; namespaces nest. `resources` gains `controller:` and `param:` (`param: :key` gives `/indicators/:key`, `params[:key]`, nested resources under `:indicator_key`, and helpers that still read `to_param`), and `resource` gains `controller:` and a block (member routes, nested singular or plural resources, Rails' paths and names: `/session/settings/edit`, `edit_session_settings_path`). `get` / `post` / ... take `on: :member` / `on: :collection` and a Symbol path (`get :refresh`); `root` inside a namespace draws `GET /admin`, `admin_root`; `jobs_page` inside a namespace takes its prefix. The module prefix applies to every controller name, given or derived. A `:param` name in a route or scope path must be a lowercase identifier.
- Decision, controllers: the controller scan follows `module Admin` nesting (and `module A::B`), so `module Admin; class ArticlesController < ApplicationController` is `Admin::ArticlesController`; the compact `class Admin::ArticlesController` is accepted too (spike item 2 compiled both). A bare superclass inside a module resolves to the module's class when one was scanned, else to the top level.
- Decision, redirects: `redirect(path, status: 301)` returns a `Gen::RedirectSpec`; `to:` takes it or a `"ctrl#action"` String. The route's handler is `Cybertrain::Router.redirect_to(ctx, "/posts/%{id}", 301)`, which fills each `%{name}` from the route's parameters, escaped as a path segment (a name the route lacks gives `""`, an unclosed `%{` stays literal). A redirect route is unnamed unless it says `as:`.
- Decision, path helpers (R-7): every generated helper takes a trailing positional `options = {}` (`def article_path(article, options = {})`, plan decision 13; a segment named `options` renames the parameter `_options`), and its body is `Cybertrain::Gen.with_options(path, options)`: `format:` adds `.ext`, `anchor:` adds `#...` last, every other key becomes a query parameter in the order given, escaped, nil leaving the key out, an Array giving `key%5B%5D=v` per item and a Hash raising `ArgumentError` (plan decision 14); values are `to_s`'d, as spec section 2.6 says. `Views.url_resolver` is a three-argument lambda, `->(name, args, options)`, and the template's `url_for` appends the same options to a String.
- Reason: Rails' vocabulary for the routes fin_dash uses; everything stays in the routes DSL that gen evaluates, and the router learns only the format (D59).
- Spinel: nested blocks keep the explicit receiver (`namespace :admin do |admin| admin.resources ... end`, plan decision 1): `Routes.draw` evaluates only its top level with `instance_eval` (D7). `scope`'s `module:` keyword is read with `binding.local_variable_get(:module)` (spikes/NOTES.md rule 95). Generated helpers take a positional Hash, not `**opts`. The block form of `redirect` would be a block stored on the route and run per request (rule 88). `scope`, `redirect` and `redirect_to` gained name-home rows (`script/check-reserved-names`; `Response#redirect` now returns nil like `Mapper#redirect`'s neighbours, rules 10 / 34).
- Rejected: `namespace` or `scope` inside a `resources` block (refused by gen: the resource modes would have to be reconciled with the scope prefixes, and the spec does not need it); keywords filling path segments (`post_path(id: 3)`: a segment is a positional argument, so a Rails port writing segment keywords rewrites them); a derived name for a redirect route (Rails derives one, but the derivation turns `/old-posts/:id` into an invalid helper argument `old-post`); `to_param` for query values (spec 2.6 says `to_s`).
- Consequences: a custom route's `:id` and `:x_id` segments keep 0.4's helper argument names (`get "/users/:id"` gives `user_path(user)`); a hand-written two-argument `url_resolver` breaks (spec section 14); a model passed as a query value prints its `to_s`.
- Verification: `test/gen_routes.rb`, `gen_routes_compiles.rb`, `gen_namespaced.rb` (a namespaced controller in the nested form with a concern), `router.rb`, `helpers.rb`; spike item 2.

### D59. Formats and `respond_to` (2026-11-11, phase 4a)

- Decision, routes: every route takes an optional extension on its last segment unless it says `format: false` (on the verbs, `resources`, `resource` and `root`; the emitter writes the argument only when false). The extension is split at the last `.`: the part before it non-empty, the tail 1 to 20 bytes of `[A-Za-z0-9_]`, counted in bytes so a multibyte segment works. A literal last segment matches whole first (`/robots.txt`). In a route that takes a format no dynamic segment captures a `.` (Rails' `[^/.?]+`, plan decision 5): `/articles/3.json.bak` is a 404. `Route#matched_extension(path_segments)` is a pure function of the segments, and `Router#call` sets `request.format_extension`.
- Decision, `request.format`: a Symbol, memoised per request. With an extension, a fixed `case` over Rails' registered extensions (`jpg` is `:jpeg`, `htm` `:html`, `txt` `:text`), `:unknown` for any other (plan decision 4). Without one, `Accept`: `:turbo_stream` when it lists `text/vnd.turbo-stream.html`, `:json` when it lists `application/json` and not `text/html` before it, else `:html`; types are compared case-insensitively and a `q=0` entry is skipped. `Request.mime_type(format)` gives the content type.
- Decision, `respond_to`: `Cybertrain::FormatCollector` (`html`, `json`, `turbo_stream`, `csv`, `xml`, `text`, `any`) runs the first matching declaration's block at the moment it is declared, so nothing is stored (plan decision 6; spike item 1); `any` never matches `:unknown`. No match answers `406 Not Acceptable` (`text/plain`, body `Not Acceptable`); a match without a block renders `<action>.<format>.erb`, html inside the controller's layout, any other format without a layout and with that format's content type. When the format is `:turbo_stream` from `Accept` and `Accept` also lists `text/html` or `*/*` (what Turbo sends with a form), an action with no `turbo_stream` declaration runs the block again for `:html`, at most one declaration's block running in all. Without `respond_to`, the implicit render looks for `<action>.<format>.erb` and answers 406 when it is missing (the html case unchanged), with the same Turbo fallback to the html template.
- Reason: Rails' behaviour, including its answer to a Turbo form post in an html-only action; JSON templates stay out with jbuilder.
- Spinel: rule 88 (no block stored on a base-class receiver), hence the immediate collector; `turbo_stream` defined on three receivers with three return types (the collector's arm, the controller's builder, the template's) and `Request#format` beside `Console::Printer.format` compile (spike items 1 and 4, rules 10 / 34). No `to_sym` on request input: Symbols are never collected, so an unregistered extension is `:unknown`. The Route objects are shared by every connection's thread, so a match keeps no state on them.
- Rejected: a generated `case request.format` rewrite of the action (not needed once spike item 1 passed); a per-controller collector storing its blocks; answering 406 to a Turbo form post of an html-only action (Rails serves html there); layouts for other formats (Rails' `layouts/application.<format>.erb` is not looked for).
- Consequences: `render :edit` inside a block renders the template of the format that block matched, as Rails does; a blockless declaration whose template is missing raises `MissingTemplate` (500), as in Rails; the Turbo fallback reads `Accept` with `include?` and ignores a `q=0` on `text/html`, and it now covers any non-html format chosen by `Accept` that lists `text/html` or `*/*`, running the block a second time (the block's other statements run twice); `Vary: Accept` is sent when the format came from `Accept`; an id with dots needs `format: false` (spec section 14).
- Verification: `test/router.rb` (dotted, multibyte and unregistered extensions), `http_request.rb` (`Accept` with q-values and wildcards), `controller_formats.rb`, `gen_routes.rb`; spike item 1.

### D60. Controller and model concerns, and `helper_method` (2026-11-11, phase 4a)

- Decision: `cybertrain/active_support.rb` defines `ActiveSupport::Concern` with `included` and `class_methods` that do nothing, and no-op stubs of every declaration an `included` block may make. gen (`Gen::ConcernScan`) reads `app/controllers/concerns/**` (and module-only files elsewhere under `app/controllers`) and `app/models/concerns/**`, expands includes depth first, each concern once (one an ancestor includes is expanded in the ancestor only), and refuses a cycle by name (`concern cycle: A includes B includes A`) and `around_action` (plan decision 10); an `include` of a module that is not a concern (`include Comparable`) is left to Ruby, as plain code, in controllers and models alike (refused at first; amended in the final fix wave). A name is resolved as Ruby resolves the constant, against the including class's modules first (`include Gate` inside `module Admin` is `Admin::Gate`), and one that reaches no concern but whose last segment is a concern's is refused, since its `included` body would otherwise be lost without a word. It writes each concern to `gen/concerns/<path under app/>` with its `included` and `class_methods` blocks blanked line for line (`gen/app.rb` requires those copies, before the models and the controllers), and `gen/concerns.rb`, required last, which reopens every including class (inside its modules for a namespaced one) with a forwarder `def self.x(...) = Gate.x(...)` per `class_methods` method (written on the concern as `def self.x`, plan decision 12), then per concern `concern_insert_at(callbacks_index, rescues_index)`, the block's body and `concern_insert_done` (plan decision 8); a model gets `model_concern_insert_at(validators_index)` (plan decision 9). The indices count the declarations above the `include` line, so the spliced declarations run where the `include` was written. `@x =` assignments in a concern's methods are the controller's view variables, and the `run_callback`, `helper_method`, `layout` and `rate_limit` tables include its declarations.
- Decision, `helper_method`: `helper_method :a, :b` in a controller or in a concern's `included` block gives the controller `helper_method?(name)` and `call_helper_method(name, args, kwargs)`, `case` tables with the arms application helpers have (D38); `Template::Helpers#helper_call` tries them after a component's view methods and before the route helpers, so a controller's templates, and the partials and components they render, call them with arguments. gen refuses a name that is a framework helper or ends in `_path` / `_url`, one an `app/helpers` module defines, one with no `def` in the class, its scanned parents or its concerns, and one whose parameters a template cannot pass (`*rest`, `**opts`, `...`).
- Reason: Rails' layout and vocabulary; one mechanism for controller and model concerns (spec section 4.2).
- Spinel: Spinel compiles a block that never runs against the module around it and refuses the bare calls in it (spikes/NOTES.md rule 92): the stubs cover `before_action` and the other declarations, but not, say, the `where` inside a scope's lambda in a model concern, so gen keeps the blocks away from Spinel altogether. A `def` inside `class_methods` becomes an instance method of every including class (rule 93), so gen refuses a `class_methods` name that an instance method of the concern, of an including class, of its ancestors or of their concerns has, naming both places. No computed `send` (rule 1): every dispatch is a generated `case`. A `Runner.run` holding two scans of `app/controllers` stopped Spinel's type inference (rule 94), so each emitter scans for itself.
- Rejected: running the `included` block at boot (a stored block, rule 88); stubs alone (above); stubbing Relation's methods on the concern (a second return type for those names, rule 34); `ModuleName.per_page` as the documented spelling (spike item 3 showed `self.class.per_page` works); refusing a model's `include` of a module that is not a concern (the first design; Ruby's own `include` is left alone now, and a misspelt concern name is a `NameError` at load).
- Consequences: `include A, B` splices B, then A, as Ruby runs the hooks; a model concern's `class_methods` name may not be a scope of the model or one of `ModelScan::RESERVED_SCOPE_NAMES` (the instance-method clash cites `file:line`); an `include` inside a model concern's `included` body is expanded and spliced after the outer body; include positions are counted lexically, so a callback declared in a loop or a conditional above the `include` is miscounted (clamped); a model concern's callbacks run after the model's own of the same kind; `app/controllers` is scanned twice; a removed concern's `gen/concerns` file is deleted and its emptied directories go with it. D15's note that concern support was a future feature is amended.
- Verification: `test/gen_concerns.rb`, `gen_model_concerns.rb`, `gen_namespaced.rb`, `gen_controllers.rb`, `helper_method.rb`, `controller_views.rb`; spike item 3.

### D61. Controller additions and the encrypted session (2026-11-11, phase 4a)

- Decision, parameters (C-1): `Params#permit` takes a key (a String value), `key: []` (a list of Strings) and `key: [...]` (a nested level, recursively, bounded by the filter's depth, never the request's) and returns `Hash{String => Object}` (plan decision 15); a value of the wrong kind, an unlisted key and an upload are dropped, and a filter that is not an Array (or an Array inside the filter) raises `ArgumentError`, a programmer's error and not a 400. `Params#expect(article: [...])` returns the level under its one key and raises `ParameterMissing` (`"param is missing or the value is empty or invalid: article"`, answered 400) when it is absent, of the wrong kind or permits nothing; `expect(:id)` answers `{"id" => "3"}` and `expect(tags: [])` `{"tags" => [...]}`, one return type. TimeZone's private `expect` became `expect_byte` (plan decision 16).
- Decision, responses (C-2, C-3, C-9): `send_data(data, filename:, type:, disposition:, status:)` with `UploadedFile.content_disposition_utf8` (a `?`-fallback quoted name and RFC 5987's `filename*`). `fresh_when` / `stale?` set a weak ETag, `W/"<first 32 hex of SHA-256>"` of a String or of `"<table>/<to_param>-<updated_at epoch>"` for a record, each followed by the request's format and `Assets.build_digest` (a digest of the asset digests, the import map and, in a `--embed-views` build, the embedded views), and `Last-Modified`, and answer 304 to GET and HEAD only (a per-user page puts the user in the `etag:` String; a page with a CSP nonce is not made `public:`); a non-empty `If-None-Match` decides alone (RFC 9110 13.1.3), else a parseable `If-Modified-Since` (IMF-fixdate, parsed in `request.rb`'s own byte parser: the HTTP layer cannot use `Cast`). `expires_in` writes `Cache-Control`, which `fresh_when` keeps. A 304 carries no Content-Type, Content-Length or body. A response negotiated by `Accept` (no extension, an Accept header sent) carries `Vary: Accept`, merged into a Vary the action set. `STATUS_SYMBOLS` has every Rack 3 symbol and `Response::STATUS_TEXT` RFC 9110's phrases (plan decision 24), with five old aliases.
- Decision, `rate_limit` (C-4): `by:`, `with:`, `if:`, `unless:` name controller methods, reached through generated `rate_limit_key` / `rate_limit_condition?` tables and `run_callback`, and gen refuses a name with no `def`; `name:` tells two limits apart; `store: :cache` (the default) counts with `Cache.increment("rate-limit:<controller_path>:<name>:<key>:<now / within>", expires_in: within)`, fixed windows aligned to the epoch, `Retry-After = within - now % within` (plan decision 17); `store: :memory` keeps D26's per-process counter. A `name:` with `:` and a second limit of the same name in one controller's chain are refused (so no client-chosen key can land in another limit's bucket), a `by:` method answering nil or `""` raises instead of counting every client together, and a `with:` method that renders nothing is followed by the 429. `RateLimit.reset_all!` also deletes the cache's rate-limit rows. `cybertrain.rb` requires `cybertrain/cache`.
- Decision, the session and cookies (C-7, C-8, Q13): `require "cybertrain"` loads `cybertrain/encryptor`, so every program links Spinel's openssl package. `Crypto.derive_key(secret_key_base, label)` gives 32 bytes per purpose (`"cybertrain session"`, `"cybertrain signed cookie"`, `"cybertrain encrypted cookie"`, behind a fixed prefix so no derived key equals an HMAC the framework computes under the raw secret; plan decision 18). The session cookie is `Base64.urlsafe_encode64(Encryptor#seal_bytes(JSON, "session"), padding: false)`, AES-256-GCM with a fresh 12-byte nonce and the purpose as additional data (plan decision 19); a value that is empty, over 4096 bytes, not Base64, not authentic (a changed, truncated or foreign one, a 0.4 signed cookie, a `cookies.encrypted` value) or not a JSON object loads as an empty session, and a session whose cookie would pass 4096 bytes raises `Session::CookieOverflow` before any Set-Cookie. `Session.load` / `dump` take the derived key. `CookieJar` (`cookies`): plain, `permanent` (`Max-Age=631139040`), `signed` (`"<b64>--<64 hex>"`, the HMAC over `"<name>=<b64>"`, compared in constant time) and `encrypted` (additional data `"cookie:<name>"`), with `Path=/`, `HttpOnly`, `SameSite=Lax` and `Secure` like the session cookie; writes replace an earlier Set-Cookie of the same name (`Response#replace_cookie`), and a value written is the value read in that request (plan decision 20).
- Decision, transport (C-5, C-11): `Cybertrain::ContentSecurityPolicy` (one method per directive, Symbols for the keywords and schemes, String sources checked for spaces, `;`, `,` and control bytes at declaration) is declared in `config/app.rb`, and a controller's `content_security_policy do |p|` copies its nearest ancestor's or the application's policy, runs the block at once and keeps the copy by class name (plan decision 7). The `ContentSecurityPolicyHeader` middleware, installed only when a policy is declared, writes `Content-Security-Policy` (or `-Report-Only`) with a per-request nonce (16 random bytes, made once per Context) in the nonce directives, and none on a 304, whose headers would replace a cached page's. `Response.default_permissions_policy` (`camera=(), microphone=(), geolocation=()`, plan decision 23) joins the default headers. `SslRedirect` (plan decision 21): https by `X-Forwarded-Proto` (its first element), read whatever `trusted_proxy` says; 301 for GET / HEAD, 307 otherwise, to the request's `Host` once it passes `Config.host_name?` (else 400); HSTS `max-age=63072000; includeSubDomains`; the health path's GET / HEAD exempt; `Config#secure_cookies?` (session_secure or force_ssl). Host checks: `CYBERTRAIN_HOSTS` entries are appended to `allowed_hosts` after the loopback names, the check runs in development or when they are set (`Config#host_check?`, plan decision 22), the health path's GET / HEAD passes, and a refused Host is logged with its bytes escaped.
- Reason: what Rails gives every controller; the session encrypted by default as Rails' cookie store is (Q13), with no new secret.
- Spinel: rule 62 framework-wide once openssl is in every program (spike item 4): every byte scanner reads its parameter through a fresh local, and the grep for a boxed `getbyte` / `each_byte` dispatch prints 0 for test/application.rb's program and the blog's server after every task. One return type per name (rules 10 / 34 / 41): `permit` widens once to `Hash{String => Object}`; `expect` coexists with Mailer's (spike item 4); the template's `form_action` became `form_record_url` because the policy's `form_action` returns nil. `if:` / `unless:` are read with `binding.local_variable_get` (rule 95). The policy block runs once (rule 88). Messages are built into locals before `raise` (rule 58); polymorphic containers are seeded (rule 9). The two-process rate-limit test spawns its own binary and reaps it (rule 75).
- Rejected: a signed-only session (readable by the client); a new key variable (keys derive from `secret_key_base`, so rotating it signs everyone out as before); per-process counts by default (a deployment of N processes was N times looser, D26); requiring a trusted peer for `X-Forwarded-Proto` (every request behind an undeclared proxy would loop; a forged header only skips the forger's own redirect); a second hosts list (spec C-11 extends `allowed_hosts`); an automatic ETag on every response.
- Consequences: every 0.4 session ends once and `Session.load` / `dump` with the raw secret raise `Encryptor::Error`; every application links OpenSSL (the generated Dockerfile's build stage has `libssl-dev`, its runtime stage `libssl3t64`; macOS needs Homebrew's OpenSSL); `params.permit` values need narrowing before String methods; SessionStore builds a jar per request; a jar cookie over 4096 bytes of `name=value` raises `Session::CookieOverflow`, and `path:` / `same_site:` are written as given (CR and LF refused). D8's session paragraph, D25's and D26's are amended.
- Verification: `test/params.rb`, `send_data.rb`, `http_caching.rb`, `controller_status.rb`, `http_response.rb`, `rate_limit.rb`, `rate_limit_store.rb` (two processes), `session.rb`, `cookie_jar.rb`, `openssl_dispatch.rb`, `csp.rb`, `force_ssl.rb`, `host_authorization.rb`, `config_settings.rb`; spike item 4.

### D62. View additions: number helpers, forms, layouts, Turbo helpers, `time_tag` and `current_page?`, collections (2026-11-11, phase 4a)

- Decision, numbers (V-6): `Cybertrain::NumberHelper`'s six module functions (`number_with_precision`, `number_with_delimiter`, `number_to_human`, `number_to_percentage`, `number_to_currency`, `number_to_human_size`) are also template helpers with Rails' keywords. They take an Integer, a Float or a numeric String and round half up on the digits of its decimal text, never `Float#round` (plan decision 27), so both runtimes print the same; anything else raises `ArgumentError` naming the helper, and so does a precision outside 0 to 100 or an exponent past 400 (a request-supplied value must not build a string of that many digits). A nil renders `""` in a template (the wrappers answer before the formatter, as 0.4's `number_with_delimiter` did), while the module functions called from Ruby raise for it. Formats are English until 4b reads them from the locale.
- Decision, forms (V-7): `form_with` gains `scope:`, `data:` (validated attribute names), `local:` (accepted, ignored: Turbo decides, plan decision 26) and `model: [:admin, @article]`; the builder gains `select` (values, `[text, value]` pairs, groups and Hashes for `<optgroup>`, `include_blank:`, `prompt:`, `selected:`, `disabled:`, `multiple:` with its hidden empty input), `collection_select`, `radio_button`, the other input types and `check_box(include_hidden: false)`; the standalone `select_tag`, `options_for_select`, `options_from_collection_for_select`, `label_tag`, `text_field_tag`, `hidden_field_tag`, `submit_tag` share its option and attribute builders. Every attribute value and option text is escaped once, a SafeString as it is; the attribute order stays the house order, options are joined with `"\n"` and written `<option selected="selected" value="x">` (plan decision 25).
- Decision, layouts (V-9): gen reads a controller's (and its concerns') `layout "x"`, `layout false` and `layout :method` lines, with `only:` / `except:`, and writes `controller_layout(action)`; the first applicable declaration wins, a method answering nil falls back to the parent's layout, and any other argument (`nil`, a lambda, a constant) is refused. `render layout:` takes `true`, `false` or a name (a leading `layouts/` is stripped, as Rails' `_normalize_layout` does); a named layout that does not exist raises `MissingTemplate`, except the default `application`, which stays optional; non-html formats render without a layout (D59).
- Decision, Turbo (V-10): `turbo_frame_tag(id_or_record, src:, loading:, target:)` writes each attribute only when given (plan decision 26); in templates `turbo_stream.replace / update / append / prepend / before / after / remove` is a `Template::TurboStreamBuilder` reached through the interpreter, a block being rendered by the helpers before the call; in controllers `turbo_stream` is a `Cybertrain::TurboStreams` and `render turbo_stream:` takes one element or an Array, as `text/vnd.turbo-stream.html`. Both builders take exactly one of `partial:` (with `locals:`), `html:` and (in templates) a block.
- Decision, V-11 and V-13: `time_tag(time, text = nil, format:)` writes the xmlschema `datetime` and formats the text with `:short`, `:long` or a strftime pattern; `current_page?(path)` answers for GET and HEAD, ignoring a trailing slash, comparing the query only when the argument has one; `image_tag` is D63's. `render partial:, collection:, as:, spacer_template:, locals:` renders once per item with `<as>_counter` (given to a strict-locals partial only when it declares it); `render @records` picks each record's `<plural>/_<singular>` partial; a component whose one required keyword is named after it gets a generated `Klass.with_collection(items)` (`render component: "comment", collection: @comments`), and gen refuses a hand-written `def self.with_collection`.
- Reason: the helpers fin_dash's views use, in Rails' spelling and output.
- Spinel: `f.select` has no public Ruby method: `select` exists on Kernel and on every Enumerable with other return types (rule 34), so the template's `f.select` runs a private `select_field` (it is reachable from templates only). `TurboStreams#update` beside `Model#update` and the other action names have name-home rows. A strftime with a built pattern on a value that may be a Date returns the pattern (rule 96), so `time_tag` calls a literal strftime per directive and raises on an unknown one rather than pass it through. A component's name reaches the stored builder lambda as a fresh local (rule 97). Byte scanners read through fresh locals (rule 62). No block is stored (rule 88).
- Rejected: Rails' "a later `layout` replaces an earlier one" (the spec allows several declarations with `only:` / `except:`, and the first applicable is easier to read; listed under the differences from Rails); `data-disable-with` and CDATA around `javascript_tag`'s content; `fields_for` and nested attributes (no `accepts_nested_attributes_for`, spec section 13).
- Consequences: `f.check_box`'s hidden `"0"` has no `autocomplete="off"` (Rails 7 writes one); `time_tag` raises on a width or flag its strftime would read differently (`%6L`, `%-T`, `%:Y`) instead of printing something else, and a width on a number replaces its padding as in CRuby; `collection:` refuses another keyword next to it (a block's content is still dropped).
