- Verification: `test/number_helper.rb`, `helpers.rb`, `form_builder.rb`, `form_tags.rb`, `controller_views.rb`, `gen_controllers.rb`, `turbo_helpers.rb`, `collections.rb`, `component_render.rb`, `gen_components.rb`.

### D63. Assets and the import map (2026-11-11, phase 4a)

- Decision, the build: every directory under `app/assets/`, then `app/javascript/` and `vendor/javascript/`, is a root, and a file's logical path is its path below its root; one found under two roots stops gen, naming both (plan decision 29). `spin run gen` writes each file as `public/assets/<name>-<digest>.<ext>`, the digest the first 8 hex characters of its SHA-256 (plan decision 2), once, through a temporary file and a rename; dotfiles and symlinked directories are skipped, a symlinked file leading out of its tree or nowhere stops gen, and so does a path with a space or a control character. A stylesheet's `url()` naming an asset is rewritten to its digested path, resolved against the stylesheet's logical directory as Propshaft resolves it (a leading `/` from the roots), its `?query` / `#fragment` kept; stylesheets are digested in dependency order and a cycle is refused; `data:`, `http(s):`, `//`, `#` and `%23` are left alone, and any other target that is not an asset is left as written with a warning. `gen/assets.rb` holds the logical-to-digested `case`, the import map's JSON and the preload list, and installs them in `Cybertrain::Assets`; the kept digests (the current one and the two before it per logical path; older files deleted) are listed in `public/assets/.manifest`, so `gen/assets.rb` stays a pure function of the sources and `--check` compares only it (plan decision 3). `Cybertrain::AssetManifest` is the one reader of `.manifest` and of digested names, for gen and for boot.
- Decision, the import map: `config/importmap.rb` is read as text: `pin name, to:, preload:` (a `to:` without a scheme is a logical asset path, a pin without `to:` names `<name>.js`) and `pin_all_from dir, under:, preload:` (`index.js` named after the directory); `preload:` defaults to true (plan decision 28); any other line stops gen.
- Decision, at run time: `asset_path`, `stylesheet_link_tag(name, media:, nonce:)`, `javascript_include_tag(name, nonce:)`, `image_tag(source, alt:, size:, class:)` and `javascript_importmap_tags(entry)`; a URL with a scheme, `//`, `data:` or `cid:`, and a path starting with `/`, are written as given. An unknown logical name raises `Assets::UnknownAsset` in development and test and, in production (`Assets.raise_on_unknown = false` at boot), is logged once (at most 1,000 names) and written undigested. `Static` serves a file under `/assets/` whose name carries a current or kept digest (`Assets.kept_file?`, the `.manifest` read at boot) with `Cache-Control: public, max-age=31536000, immutable`, everything else as before, no file whose real path leaves `public/` and no dotfile under `/assets` (`.manifest`, a crashed run's temp file; `/.well-known` is still served). Gen stops at an asset root (`app/assets`, `app/javascript`, `vendor/javascript`) that is a symlink. The development loop watches the three asset roots (`Dev::WATCHED`, now in `cybertrain/dev/watched.rb`). `cybertrain new` writes `app/assets/stylesheets/application.css` (formerly `public/style.css`), `app/javascript/application.js`, `config/importmap.rb`, the layout's `csp_meta_tag` / `stylesheet_link_tag` / `javascript_importmap_tags`, `/public/assets/` in `.gitignore`, the commented CSP block, OpenSSL in the Dockerfile (D61) and `asset_path: /app/public/assets` in `config/deploy.yml`.
- Reason: Rails 8's Propshaft and importmap-rails layout, with the manifest compiled into the binary so the deployed program needs only `public/assets/` beside it.
- Spinel: the manifest is a generated `case` (rule 1; spike item 5: a 200-arm `case` compiles in 1.3 s and answers in 293 ns, and SHA-256 of 1 MB takes 0.8 ms compiled). The resolver lambda is made by a method, not written as a literal into the module's ivar (rule 82); module state lives in module-level ivars (rule 18); byte scanners read through fresh locals (rule 62); a `[String, Array<String>]` pair returned and destructured compiles.
- Rejected: the kept-digest history in `gen/assets.rb` (`--check` would depend on the build's past); resolving `url()` against the file's directory under `app/assets/` (Propshaft resolves logical paths, so `url("../images/logo.svg")` from `stylesheets/` climbs out of the roots and is not an asset); `bin/importmap pin` and bundling Turbo (spec section 13); serving through a symlink out of `public/`; ETags on digested files (a digested file never changes).
- Consequences: `public/assets/` is build output, git-ignored, and `cybertrain build` and the image build run gen; a fresh image carries only the current digests, which Kamal's `asset_path` bridges; a Capistrano-style deploy that symlinks `public/assets` must mount it instead; a reverse proxy serving `public/` itself must add the immutable header; dotfiles under `/assets` (`.manifest`, a crashed run's temp file) are not served; examples/blog's stylesheet moved to `app/assets/stylesheets/application.css` with `url("logo.svg")`.
- Verification: `test/gen_assets.rb` (a fixture with a compiled, populated `gen/assets.rb`), `assets.rb`, `static.rb`, `dev_watcher.rb`, `cli_new.rb`; examples/blog's tests fetch the digested stylesheet and check the immutable header; spike item 5.

### D64. Shared state has one owner each, and the Application lifecycle is a contract (2026-11-11, issue #180)

- Context: `Application` takes a `Config`, yet `boot` wrote process-wide settings into `Views`, `Assets`, `Multipart`, `BlobStore`, `Params`, `Request`, `ParameterFilter`, `Response` and the logger, and `DB` holds one pool for the process. What happens on a second boot, on a second `Application`, after a stop, and what a test must put back between cases, was whatever the code happened to do.
- Decision: the framework's state is one of four kinds, and each kind has one owner and one period in which it may change.

  | Kind | What | Owner (who writes it) | Set | May change | Ends |
  |---|---|---|---|---|---|
  | Configuration | `Cybertrain.config` (a `Config`) | the application: the environment, then `config/app.rb` | when the program loads | until `Application#boot`; after it only what a role forces (`worker`, a port argument) | with the process |
  | Registrations | `Job` handlers, `Scheduler::TASKS`, `Background` services and feeds, `Main.command` commands, `Views.helpers_builder` / `component_builder`, `Assets.install`'s tables, `Config.encryption_required`, `ContentSecurityPolicy` controller policies | the files that load: `gen/*.rb`, `app/**`, `require "cybertrain/websocket"` | at load time | not after boot | never reset (a process is one program) |
  | Settings | `Views` (engine, layout, URL resolver), `Assets` (kept digests, raise on unknown), `Multipart` limits, `BlobStore.root`, `Params.max_page`, `Request.trusted_proxy` / `trusted_proxies`, `ParameterFilter.names`, `Response.default_permissions_policy`, the logger's level | the booting `Application`, through `Application.configure_views` and `Runtime.apply` and nothing else | `Application#boot` | by the next boot (the last boot wins, whole); a test may set one directly | `Runtime.reset!` restores the defaults |
  | Resources | the `DB` pool; the HTTP server; the threads of `Job`, `Scheduler`, `Background`, `Stream::Poller`; the TERM/INT/HUP handlers | whoever acquired it: a pool belongs to the boot that connected it (or to the caller who called `DB.connect`); a role's threads and handlers belong to that role | `boot` (the pool), `serve` / `run_worker` / `run_feeds` (the rest) | never shared between two roles | the role stops its own threads and gives the handlers back before it returns; `Application#shutdown` closes a pool its boot connected |
  | Request state | `Context`, `Request`, `Response`, the controller, the session, the transaction a thread holds (`DB.with` inside `DB.transaction`) | the request's thread | per request | within the request | with the request |

- One process runs one `Application` at a time. That is now said rather than implied: the settings are the process's, so two Applications with different configs cannot serve side by side, and several are not a goal (D35 runs several processes instead). What is supported, because tests and `Main` do it, is one Application after another.
- The lifecycle contract:
  - `boot` validates, connects the database unless something already has, and installs the settings. It answers `self`; `booted?` is true afterwards.
  - Booting twice (the same Application, or a second one) is defined: every setting is written again from the booting Application's config, so the settings after a boot depend on that boot alone. The pool is kept: a boot never replaces a connected pool (the first boot's, or the caller's).
  - `shutdown` (new) closes the pool only if this Application's boot connected it and it is still that pool (`DB.generation`); it is a no-op otherwise and when repeated. `boot` works again after it. The settings stay until the next boot or `Runtime.reset!`.
  - `serve`, `run_worker` and `run_feeds` stop every thread they started and restore the signal handlers they replaced before they return; the Application stays booted, and a role may be run again (`test/main_roles.rb` runs one after another). One role runs at a time (`Runtime.enter_role`): each owns the process-wide service threads and the signal handlers.
  - `Test::Client`, `console`, `migrate`, `db` and `Main.command` boot (or only configure views) and start nothing (D39).
- `Cybertrain::Runtime` (cybertrain/runtime.rb) is the one writer of the settings: `Runtime.apply(config)` (what `boot` used to do inline, every setting on every call), `Runtime.reset!` (every setting back to its default; for tests), `Runtime.describe` (all of them as one line, so a test compares two states with one assertion), `Runtime.applied`, `Runtime.role`. The modules keep their own setters (`Params.max_page=` and the like stay usable from a test), and `Cybertrain.config` and `DB.with` stay the entry points: `Runtime` holds no copy of a setting, so nothing is managed twice.
- Tests: there was no single way to put the settings back, so a case could pass or fail by what booted before it. `Runtime.reset!` is that way, and `test/runtime.rb` checks the order independence directly: boot A then B and boot B alone leave the same `Runtime.describe`, and the reverse; a reset after any boot equals the state before the first.
- Known gap, kept on purpose: an `Application` built with `config:` other than `Cybertrain.config` installs its own config's settings, while code with no Application at hand (`Job`, `Mailer`, `Stream`, `Encryptor`, `HttpClient`, `Password`, the template helpers) reads `Cybertrain.config`. `Main` always passes the global one, so only tests see two; a test that needs those modules to follow assigns `Cybertrain.config = c` itself. Making boot assign it would change what existing tests observe, and moving the readers onto an injected runtime object is the "execution environment object" the issue leaves for when several Applications become a goal.
- Rejected: a `Runtime` object passed to every reader (every `Params`, `Request`, `Multipart` call site would need one, and under Spinel a module-level ivar is the cheap, typed way to share a value: rules 18, 64); refusing a second boot (the test suite boots dozens of Applications per process); resetting the registrations too (they are written by `require`, which runs once); closing the pool at the end of `serve` (a process that served is about to exit, and an in-process caller may still read the database).
- Verification: `test/runtime.rb` (the defaults, a boot's settings, reset, order independence, double boot, `shutdown` and pool ownership, the role claim); `test/application.rb`, `test/main_roles.rb` and the rest of the suite run unchanged.

### D76. Spinel workarounds are indexed by shape, and a compiler update has a checklist (2026-11-11, issue #192)

- Context: spikes/NOTES.md numbers the Spinel rules in the order they were found and docs/spinel-feedback.md follows what went upstream; both are complete and neither answers "which shapes of the framework exist because of Spinel, which of those are permanent, and what must be re-checked when the pin moves". A general-purpose refactoring that is correct Ruby can stop the framework compiling, and nothing said that a CRuby pass is not acceptance.
- Decision: no new record, an index over the existing ones. spikes/NOTES.md gains "Workarounds that shape the design, and what a Spinel update re-checks": (1) the permanent shapes, which are API policy that follows from ahead-of-time compilation (explicit receivers, generated code, one return type per method, module-level ivars, typed seeds) with the decision and the test for each; (2) the workarounds tied to a build (rescue placement, the JSON rescue order, byte scanners, lambdas returned by methods, stored blocks and subclass overrides, the separate generator passes, sockets, `SPINEL_WORKERS`, `exit` on the main thread) with the rule, the files, the test that fails without it and its status on the pin; (3) the steps of a pin update (everything compiled, the benchmark, re-check the "still there" rows, read the release notes, remove a workaround only in its own change with its test); (4) what CRuby cannot tell you; (5) what a built binary still depends on at run time (OS and libc, `libsqlite3` and the versions the framework's SQL needs, OpenSSL 3, zlib, optional jemalloc, zoneinfo, a local file system), in step with docs/guide/install.md and the deploy tutorial.
- CONTRIBUTING.md and the pull request template say it as a rule: a structural change (code moved between methods, classes or files, a new abstraction, different generator output) is accepted only when the affected tests pass compiled under Spinel.
- Rejected: a second document repeating the rules (they would drift); renumbering the rules by topic (they are cited by number across the repository); a script that greps for workarounds (a shape is not greppable; the tests named in the index are the check).

### D74. One SQLite file for every service: the storage boundaries, their transaction guarantees and the measured interference (2026-11-11, issue #190)

- Context: business tables, `cybertrain_jobs`, `cybertrain_cache` (the `rate_limit` counters among its rows), `cybertrain_messages`, `cybertrain_leases`, `cybertrain_schedules` and `cybertrain_feeds` share the application's database (D10, D37, D39, D52, D54, D56). That gives one file to back up and "enqueue iff the order committed", and it makes every service's write compete for SQLite's one writer. Until now nobody had measured how much, and which guarantees depend on the sharing was written down only per service.
- Decision: everything stays in the one file. No schema, API or behaviour changes. What this decision adds is the measurement ([bench/mixed](../bench/mixed), [bench/mixed_load.rb](../bench/mixed_load.rb)), the document that records it, the scale it supports and how to observe the interference ([docs/sqlite-shared-load.md](sqlite-shared-load.md)), and the boundary table below.
- The measurement: one compiled program on one WAL database with the framework's pool and the real poller, scheduler and job threads runs business transactions, primary-key reads, `Job.enqueue` (the job threads run the jobs), `Cache.write` / `read`, the `Cache.increment` of `rate_limit`, and `Stream.broadcast` (the poller hands the rows to a subscriber), each alone and then together, and prints operations, latency percentiles, job delay (enqueue to start), stream delay (broadcast to hand-off) and the calls that raised `database is locked`. Recorded on 2026-11-11 on a busy shared machine, so indicative: about 500 to 750 commits a second in all (each commit syncs the WAL, `synchronous = FULL`); the services divide that budget, so the business writers kept a half to a sixth of their throughput in the mixed phase; nothing failed, and the cost is tail latency (a business transaction's p99 from 8 ms to 64 ms on two workers, to 0.7 s at saturation); reads slow down too, on one worker because an FFI call holds it (NOTES rule 80) and at saturation because writers waiting for the lock keep the pool's connections; the job delay is `job_poll_interval` (0.7 to 0.8 s at the median even alone) and the stream delay `stream_poll_ms` plus the wait for a commit.
- The storage boundaries. "Joins" means the module's writes go through `DB.with`, which inside a `DB.transaction` on the same thread uses that transaction's connection (`Pool#with`, `held_index`), so the write commits or rolls back with the caller's rows. bench/mixed_load.rb checks it on every run for a business row, a job, a message and two cache rows (none after a rollback, all after a commit); `test/jobs.rb` has the job case.

| store | tables | all SQL lives in | joins the caller's transaction, and what relies on it | durability | if it moved out of the file |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| business data | the application's tables, `schema_migrations` | the generated models, `Relation`, `DB`; `DB::Migrator` | it is the transaction | must survive | not considered |
| jobs | `cybertrain_jobs` | `Job` (the queue, claim, outcome, recovery), `JobsPage` (the lists and the retry / discard / delete actions on failed and discarded rows) | yes: `Job.enqueue`, `perform_later` and `Mailer.deliver_later` in a transaction exist iff it commits. The claim is its own `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` transaction (two claimers never take one row or the last concurrency slot); the handler's writes and the outcome are separate commits, so a job runs at least once | must survive: a lost `queued` row is lost work | enqueue-in-transaction is lost: define the outbox (a business-side row and a relay), the duplicate rule for a relay that retries, and what `Job.enqueue` does when the queue is unreachable (raise, or queue locally) |
| scheduler timetable | `cybertrain_schedules` | `Scheduler::Timetable` | not used in one: written after a task ran. A crash between the run and the write can run a cron slot again on takeover | should survive (a lost row means a missed slot is not caught up) | nothing transactional is lost; it must stay readable by every process that can lead |
| cache, rate-limit counters | `cybertrain_cache` | `Cache`; `RateLimit.clear_shared_counts` (one `DELETE` of the `rate-limit:` key range) | yes, which matters as a hazard, not a guarantee: a `Cache.write` or a `fetch` miss inside a transaction that rolls back is not stored. `rate_limit` counts in a before-action, outside any transaction. `increment` and `write(unless_exist: true)` are atomic single statements, across processes | safe to lose, with two cautions: losing the counters reopens every window, and a `write(unless_exist: true)` used as a lock is released by the loss | the first candidate to move (a second SQLite file would take its commits off the writer lock). Define: reads and writes when the store is unavailable (a miss and a no-op, or an error; `rate_limit` open or closed), and that nothing may depend on a cache write rolling back |
| stream messages | `cybertrain_messages` | `Stream` (the poller and the hub call it, and run no SQL of their own) | yes: a broadcast in a transaction is delivered iff it commits. The ids are the delivery order and the replay cursor (`Last-Event-ID`); with one writer, id order is commit order, so the poller never passes an id that commits later | safe to lose after `stream_retention` (60 s); inside it a loss is a missed live update, and the client reloads | broadcast-in-transaction is lost: define whether a message may be delivered for a rolled-back change or dropped for a committed one, who assigns the order and the replay cursor, and what the endpoint does when the store is down (today: 503 until the poller runs) |
| leases | `cybertrain_leases` | `Lease` | not used in one. `acquire` is one upsert: of the processes sharing the file exactly one holds a name, because they share the writer | safe to lose (the next tick takes it again); the cost of a loss is two leaders until the old one's next renewal | it must be the same store for every process; an external one needs expiry by its clock and a rule for a leader that cannot reach it (stop leading) |
| feed status | `cybertrain_feeds` | `WebSocket::FeedTable` | not used in one | safe to lose (rewritten every `feed_status_interval`); `/up` reads it | `/up` and `feeds status` need a reader for it, and a rule for "unreadable" (today: not stale, one WARN) |

- The rule for a store outside the file: none is added now. One may be added (a second SQLite file, or an external service) only together with a written definition, in this document, of (1) its consistency with the business transaction: what replaces "in the same commit", from the table's last column; (2) unavailability: what each public call does when the store cannot be reached, and whether a request fails or degrades; (3) partial failure: the business commit succeeded and the store's write did not, and the reverse; (4) ordering and duplicates, for jobs and messages; (5) how the operator sees each of those. A second SQLite file attached to the same connection does not keep the guarantee either: in WAL mode a transaction across attached databases is atomic per file, not across them.
- Reason: the measured ceiling is far above the applications CyberTrain is for (tens of writing requests and jobs a second), the same-commit guarantees are used by the documented patterns (enqueue and broadcast from an action after a save), and each store that leaves needs a failure design that the single file makes unnecessary. The cheap levers come first and are in the document: do the service writes inside the request's transaction (one commit instead of several), `store: :memory` for limits that may be per process, fewer cache writes on request paths, batched broadcasts, a larger `pool_size`.
- Rejected: moving the cache to its own file now (its writes and the rate-limit counts were half of run A's mixed commits, but it is a behaviour change, needs the definitions above and a decision on backup and `db` commands, and no application has reported the limit); `PRAGMA synchronous = NORMAL` as part of this change (the largest single lever, since every commit is a sync; it changes what survives a power loss, so it needs its own decision and measurement); counting lock waits in `Connection#step_waiting` now (proposed in the document; it adds state to the per-statement path, which must be measured under two workers first, NOTES rule 57); a `.expected` snapshot for the bench (its numbers are not deterministic); `CacheStore` / `JobStore` / `MessageStore` classes (each store's SQL already sits in one module, two for jobs; an interface with one implementation would be an abstraction for a move that is not planned).
- Consequences: the limits are known and stated per deployment as a commit budget; an operator has the request log, the services' error lines, `/up`, `jobs list`, the jobs page, SQL and `bench/mixed`, and no direct count of lock waits (the proposed counter). a split of `cybertrain/job.rb` into several files does not change the table: the owner stays the job modules.
- Verification: `bench/mixed duration=5`, `... workers=2` and the saturation run of docs/sqlite-shared-load.md, compiled with `spinel bench/mixed_load.rb --strict-require -I . -O 1` (Spinel 2026.11.04.7026); each run printed `transaction coupling: ok (rows after a rollback: 0, after a commit: 5 of 5)` and no busy or error count. `script/gate` passes; no test or snapshot changed.

### D65. Config: checks that only read, and file operations kept apart (2026-11-11, issue #181)

- Context: `Config` (1,700 lines) held the settings of every area (web, database, jobs, feeds, stream, security), the checks boot runs on them, the resolution of the secret (which reads, creates and chmods `tmp/secret_key`) and a `mkdir -p`. `Application#boot` called fourteen `*_error` methods one by one, with `resolve_secret!` after them. Nothing said which of these may touch the disk, so "is this configuration usable" could not be asked without preparing the disk for it.
- Decision: the boundary is the side effect, not the class.
  - `cybertrain/config/validation.rb` holds every check (`session_same_site_error`, `csrf_exempt_prefixes_error`, `max_body_bytes_error`, `upload_limits_error`, `scheduler_error`, `max_page_error`, `trusted_proxies_error`, `health_error`, `feeds_error`, `stream_error`, `security_error`, `encryption_error`: one per area, unchanged, under their public names) and `Config#validation_error`, which asks them in the order boot always did and answers the first message or `""`. The contract of that file: a check reads the Config (and, for `scheduler_tz`, the system's zone files) and does nothing else: no database connection, no file or directory created, no secret resolved.
  - `cybertrain/config/files.rb` (`Cybertrain::ConfigFiles`, private) holds the file operations: `load_or_create_secret(path, length)` and `make_directory`. `Config#resolve_secret!` keeps its name and its behaviour and is now two lines over it; `Config.make_directory` stays for its callers (`DB::CLI`).
  - `Application#boot` states the order (D66): `validation_error`, then the registrations' check (`Scheduler.job_kinds_error`), then `resolve_secret!`, then the database.
- Every setting name, default, environment variable and message is unchanged; the areas' methods are the ones tests already call (`test/application.rb`, `test/config_settings.rb`, `test/health.rb`, `test/boot_encryption.rb`). One ordering changed, on purpose: a cron task naming an unregistered job kind is now reported after all of the Config's own problems rather than between `scheduler_error` and `max_page_error` (it is a registration's problem, not the Config's), so a boot with both problems names the Config's first.
- Spinel: the class is reopened in a second file rather than handed to a validator object: each check reads ivars whose types the class already settles (rules 7, 9), and a `validate(config)` taking the Config through a parameter would read the same values through accessors for no gain. The module-level `@encryption_required` and its accessors stay in config.rb, where the class body first runs (rule 18).
- Rejected: one `Config` class per area (`WebConfig`, `JobConfig`, ...) behind `config.jobs.threads` (every setting name applications and the guides use would change, for a split that the per-area check methods already give the validation); collecting every error instead of the first (boot prints one line and exits; a list is a different contract for `cybertrain doctor` to ask for when it needs it); resolving the secret lazily in `SessionStore` (the first request would create a file, and a read-only deploy would fail there instead of at boot).
- Verification: `test/config_validation.rb` (a valid default; the order; each area's boundary values through `validation_error`; validation with paths that do not exist leaves no file, no directory and no database connection; `resolve_secret!` creates the file once, mode 0600, and refuses production without a secret), next to the existing per-area tests.

## 5. Request flow

1. `Server` parses HTTP/1.1 on the accepted socket and builds a `Request`.
2. Middleware chain: logging -> static files -> `_method` override -> session (decoding the signed cookie; decrypting the encrypted one since 2026-11-11, D61) -> CSRF verification -> router. Since 2026-11-11 the host check and the https redirect come first when configured, and the Content Security Policy header is written on the way out (D61).
3. The router walks the route table in `gen/routes.rb` and calls the dispatch clause of the matching route: `PostsController.new(ctx).process(:show) { |c| c.show }`.
4. `process` runs the `before_action`s in order (the symbol form via the `case` in the generated `run_callback(name)`, the block form via `block.call(self)` with the controller as the argument; the chain stops as soon as one renders or redirects), calls the action body, implicitly renders the template named after the action if neither a render nor a redirect happened, and finally runs the `after_action`s.
5. render passes `view_assigns` (generated), the locals and the helpers to the template engine, renders in an environment of plain polymorphic values, and wraps the result in the layout.
6. The `Response` is written back to the socket. With keep-alive, it waits for the next request.

## 6. Generator inputs and outputs

| Input | Output | Content |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `db/schema.rb` | `gen/models/<model>.rb` | `attr_accessor`, the `case` for `read_attribute` / `write_attribute` / `assign_attributes`, `from_row`, `to_json`, `attribute_names`, associations derived from foreign keys, `<Model>Relation` |
| `config/routes.rb` | `gen/routes.rb` | the route table, the dispatch `case`, `*_path` / `*_url`, the polymorphic path `case` |
| `app/controllers/**/*.rb` (lexical scan) | `gen/controllers.rb` | per-controller `view_assigns` and the `run_callback(name)` `case` (open class) |
| `app/models/**/*.rb` (lexical scan) | appended to `gen/models/<model>.rb` | per-model `call_view_method(name)` `case` (argument-less `def` only) |
| `app/controllers/concerns/**`, `app/models/concerns/**` (lexical scan) | `gen/concerns.rb`, `gen/concerns/**` | the concerns' `included` blocks spliced into each including class, `class_methods` forwarders, and the concern modules with those blocks blanked (2026-11-11, D60) |
| `app/assets/**`, `app/javascript/**`, `vendor/javascript/**`, `config/importmap.rb` | `gen/assets.rb`, `public/assets/` | the logical-to-digested `case`, the import map and its preloads; the digested files and `.manifest` (2026-11-11, D63) |
| file list of `app/` | `gen/app.rb` | list of `require_relative` |

All generated code is committed. CI runs `spin run gen` and then `git status --porcelain --untracked-files=all -- gen/`, which must print nothing: a changed file, a file that is gone and a file never committed all fail it (`git diff --exit-code` did not see the last, issue #205).

The inputs above are what gen reads when it runs. What it is compiled from is a different set: whatever `bin/gen.rb` reaches through its requires, which `config/routes.rb` can extend with `app/routes/extra.rb` or a package called through `Package.draw(r)`.

The development rebuild (D12) uses that difference. spin recompiles an executable when any Ruby file of the package or of a path dependency changes, so an edit to a controller used to spend about 3 s of an 8 s rebuild compiling a generator identical to the one on disk. So `Dev::Rebuilder` first asks the compiler what gen is now: `spinel bin/gen.rb -c -o build/gen-check.c --force --no-line-map $(spin flags)`, quietly, about 0.3 s on `examples/blog`. If that succeeds, `build/bin/gen` exists and the C is byte-identical to `build/gen-compiled.c`, the C saved from the last successful full rebuild, the rebuild runs `./build/bin/gen` instead of `spin run gen`. The rebuild always runs gen, because a controller edit can change `gen/controllers.rb`.

The check compares the compiler's output because only the compiler knows what is compiled in. An earlier version followed `require`s by regex and signed the files it found, and disagreed with the compiler on `require File.join(__dir__, "..", "app", "routes", "x")`, which is folded and compiled but was not followed: the stale gen then wrote wrong `gen/` output with no error. Comparing C needs no list of inputs, so there is none to get wrong. `--no-line-map` drops the `#line` directives, so a comment moved in a required file costs no compile; the 0.3 s is paid on every rebuild, and it is what buys back the 3 s. The two files live in `build/` because spin warns on every command about spinel's output anywhere else in the package, and because they should go when `build/bin/gen` goes.

Any failure of the check (no `spinel` on `PATH`, `spin flags` failing, a compile error, an unreadable file) means not fresh, and the full `spin run gen` reports what is really wrong. The check runs before the build, so an edit made during the build reads as a change next time. After a successful full run the check runs again, and if the compiler still gives the same C it is renamed to the saved one and `build/gen-compiled.stamp` records the binary's modification time and size; a different C means an edit during the build may be in the binary, so nothing is saved. A direct run records nothing, nor does a failed run unless the check was fresh, when the binary came from the saved C unless such an edit says otherwise. The stamp is part of fresh: `spin run gen` by hand from edited sources, then the edit undone, leaves the C matching and a different binary. When the C matches and the stamp does not, the rebuild deletes the binary before `spin run gen`, because spin reuses a binary newer than its sources. A stale check deletes the saved C right away, because `spin run gen` replaces the binary even if the build after it fails, and undoing the edit must then not read as a match. A failed direct run is followed at once by the full command, so a broken binary never strands the loop; the price is that a compile error in the application is built twice.

What the check does not see is whatever reaches gen's binary without changing its C: a `.c` file of a path dependency, which spin compiles but gen's output does not depend on unless a generator calls into it, and the Spinel runtime, which the C only `#include`s.

## 7. Draft specification of the template language

ERB tags: `<% %>`, `<%= %>` (escaped), `<%== %>` (unescaped), `<%# %>`, `<%- -%>` (trim), and a leading `<%# locals: (a:, b:) %>`.

Expressions:

- Literals: integers, floats, strings (`"..."` allows `#{}` interpolation, `'...'` does not), symbols, `nil` / `true` / `false`, arrays `[...]`, hashes `{ key: value }` (the same form as keyword arguments)
- Variables: `@ivar` (from `view_assigns`), locals (`each` bindings and strict locals)
- Calls: `recv.method`, `recv.method(args, key: value)`, `recv&.method`, `method(args)` (helpers)
- Operators: `== != < <= > >= && || ! + - * / % ?:`
- Control: `if` / `elsif` / `else` / `unless` / `end`, `each do |x|` / `each_with_index do |x, i|`, `form_with(...) do |f|`
- Per-value table of allowed methods: String (`upcase` `downcase` `capitalize` `strip` `size` `length` `empty?` `to_s`), Integer / Float (arithmetic and `to_s`), Time (`strftime` `year` `month` `day` `to_s`), Array (`size` `length` `empty?` `first` `last` `any?`), Model (generated attributes, generated associations, `errors`, `persisted?`, `new_record?`, `to_param`, those declared with `view_methods`), `errors` (`any?` `full_messages` `[]` `count`)

Explicitly unsupported: arbitrary blocks (`map { }` and so on), local definitions other than assignment, `case`, regular expressions, ranges, method definitions, `require`. (This is the draft of 2026-09-24. Since 2026-11-05 `case` / `when`, blocks on a fixed list of Array and Hash methods and the `tag` builder exist (D9, D38); docs/guide/template-language.md describes what is built.)

## 8. What was deliberately left out

- `rails console` as a Ruby prompt (`eval` does not exist). Since 2026-11-05 `./<app> console` evaluates the template expression language over the generated models, and `./<app> dbconsole` opens `sqlite3` (D41)
- Constants and arbitrary Ruby in templates (`case`, blocks on a fixed list of methods and the `tag` builder exist since 2026-11-05, D9), compile-time detection of type errors in templates, error pages with stack traces
- A fully single-file production binary (`app/views` and `public` ship alongside it; recoverable with a future compilation backend)
- `has_many :through`, polymorphic associations, nested `includes`, i18n (`namespace`, formats and `respond_to` arrived on 2026-11-11 in D58 and D59, with concerns, the controller and view additions and assets in D60 to D63) (authentication arrived on 2026-10-22 in the form of D15: code written by the generator, plus `Cybertrain::Password`) (the `transaction` API in D20, mailers in D27 and jobs in D28 arrived on 2026-10-25 in minimal form) (the outbound WebSocket client arrived on 2026-10-26 in D33, and browser delivery over Server-Sent Events and WebSocket on 2026-11-05 in D36 and D37) (one level of `includes` arrived on 2026-11-07 in D47). Several of these are later phases of the programme in D1
- Rack compatibility, running Rails code unchanged (porting by hand is in scope since 2026-11-05, D1), running on CRuby

## 9. Unverified assumptions and spike plan (M0)

Small Spinel programs are placed in `spikes/` and verified against `2026.09.12`. (The results are in `spikes/NOTES.md`. The programs themselves were deleted after verification.)

| # | What is verified | If it fails |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | Installing `spinel` / `spin`, the optimisation levels in `spinel --help`, compile time at the scale of 2,000 lines | Re-evaluate how the development loop (D12) feels |
| 2 | Whether a non-literal `send` can dispatch to literal names, the return type, the block form of `instance_exec` | **Result**: `send` works, but the 128-literal limit makes it unusable in real applications. `instance_exec(&stored)` is not possible. -> D8 / D9 revised to the generated `case` statement approach |
| 3 | **Whether a tree-walking interpreter with a tagged union `Value` passes type inference, and whether rendering a 100-line template 10,000 times is fast enough** | **Result**: It passes. With a monomorphic `INode` AST + plain polymorphic values, 1.54 times the hand-written version and 3.2 times faster than CRuby. The `Value` wrapper was not adopted |
| 4 | Whether `first` is inferred as `Post \| nil` with a base `Relation` and `PostRelation`, without the types widening across several models | **Result**: It is inferred. Abstract stubs in the base, setters that return `nil`, and nullable attributes through cast methods are required |
| 5 | 100 concurrent connections with `TCPServer` + green threads + keep-alive, latency, whether issue #4528 reproduces | **Result**: 52-58k req/s at c=100 keep-alive (`SPINEL_WORKERS=1`), p99 ≤ 2 ms. Silent connections do not stop the others. However, always call `wait_readable(timeout)` immediately before `readpartial` (a bare `readpartial` occupies the OS worker and causes the same stall as #4528) |
| 6 | SQLite FFI open / prepare / bind / step / column, `blocking: true`, use of a `SizedQueue` pool from multiple threads, WAL | **Result**: Works. A race crash on the static `ffi_buffer` was avoided with `malloc` scratch space |
| 7 | FFI for `execv`, whether `trap` is available, `SO_REUSEADDR` | **Result**: All work (`trap("HUP")` confirmed on a real machine, an execv shim, immediate rebinding of the same port). D12 confirmed |
| 8 | HMAC with `openssl` / `digest`, `json`, `escapeHTML` from `cgi`, query parsing with `uri` | **Result**: json / uri / securerandom / base64 / strscan / stringio work. `cgi` does not exist in this tag, so HTML escaping is our own. HMAC is implemented by hand on top of `Digest::SHA256` |

go / no-go: settle D8 / D9 from the results of 2 and 3 before moving on to M1.

## 10. Milestones

- **M0** Spikes complete and the go / no-go decision.
- **M1** HTTP server, Request / Response, middleware, routing with a hand-written route table, static files, logging. A `hello world` controller works.
- **M2** Template engine, layouts, partials, helpers, `view_assigns` generation.
- **M3** SQLite adapter, migrations, `db/schema.rb` dump, model generation, validations, `Cybertrain::Test`.
- **M4** routes generation and URL helpers, form helpers, sessions, flash, CSRF. The blog works end to end and the integration tests pass in CI. **Acceptance criteria**.
- **M5** Development loop, error pages, `cybertrain new` / `generate scaffold`, README.

## 11. Repository layout

Framework (this repository):

```
cybertrain/
  spin.toml  cybertrain.rb  cybertrain.gemspec    # the gem holds only the CLI and what it requires
  cybertrain/
    main.rb  application.rb  config.rb  views.rb  version.rb   # entry point and boot for bin/<name>.rb
    db.rb  dev.rb  generator.rb  template.rb  schema.rb   # require entry points for each subdirectory
    router.rb  controller.rb  params.rb  session.rb  flash.rb  callback.rb  errors.rb  context.rb
    middleware.rb  app.rb   # Middleware base class and the default stack (App)
    http/        # server.rb  parser.rb  request.rb  response.rb  query.rb  cookies.rb
    middleware/  # request_logger  static  method_override  session_store  csrf_protection  error_pages
    template/    # lexer  parser  ast  inode  interpreter  helpers  form_builder  engine
    model.rb  relation.rb  validator.rb  cast.rb  migration.rb
    schema/      # table.rb  definition.rb  dumper.rb (the db/schema.rb DSL)
    db/          # sqlite_ffi.rb  connection.rb  pool.rb  migrator.rb  schema_dumper.rb  sqlite_ddl.rb  cli.rb  error.rb
    generator/   # runner.rb  routes_dsl.rb  *_emitter.rb  model_scan.rb  controller_scan.rb  manifest.rb  inflector.rb  url_support.rb
    dev/         # watcher.rb  rebuilder.rb  reexec.rb  error_page.rb (D12, section 6)
    cli.rb  cli/ # new_app.rb  scaffold.rb  templates.rb  build.rb
    test.rb  test/client.rb  html.rb  crypto.rb  logger.rb
  bin/cybertrain.rb  exe/cybertrain   # CLI (spin install / gem)
  script/regen-snapshot
  test/                     # spin test (.expected files are committed)
  examples/blog/            # the acceptance-criteria app
  spikes/NOTES.md           # record of the M0 spikes (the programs were deleted after verification; they are in the git history)
  README.md  docs/design.md  docs/deploy.md  docs/deploy.en.md  docs/deploy-kamal.md  docs/guide/template-language.md
  .github/workflows/ci.yml
```

App (generated by `cybertrain new blog`):

```
blog/
  spin.toml                 # [dependencies] cybertrain = ...
  spin.lock                 # written by spin lock (absent in examples/blog, which uses a path dependency)
  .gitignore  README.md
  app/controllers/  app/models/  app/views/  app/helpers/
  config/routes.rb  config/app.rb  config/deploy.yml   # deploy.yml: Kamal (D11)
  Dockerfile  .dockerignore  .kamal/secrets
  .github/workflows/ci.yml  # calls the setup-cybertrain action (D32)
  db/migrate/  db/schema.rb
  gen/                      # app.rb  controllers.rb  migrations.rb  models/  routes.rb  views.rb
  bin/blog.rb  bin/gen.rb  bin/db.rb   # blog.rb: server / migrate / db; db.rb: migrations during development
  test/                     # the skeleton has only .keep (examples/blog has articles.rb comments.rb support/blog_test.rb)
  public/  storage/  tmp/   # build/ and dist/ are in .gitignore; public/up.html is the Kamal health check
```

## 13. Implementation status (2026-09-25)

- Milestones M0-M5 in section 10 have all been reached. The acceptance criteria in section 2.2 are met by `examples/blog` (Article + Comment, server-side HTML, SQLite, a single binary with `spin build`); its 21 integration test cases and the framework's own 57 `spin test` programs pass.
- The development loop (D12) was confirmed on a real machine: view edits take effect immediately without a rebuild, and a controller edit was rebuilt in about 24 seconds (`spin run gen` + `spin build`; the build target at the time was the server bin) and replaced by the new binary via `execv`, keeping the same PID.
- The 42 Spinel constraints found during implementation are in `spikes/NOTES.md`. User-facing explanations are in `README.md` and `docs/guide/template-language.md`.
- Known gaps: the default value syntax for strict locals (`<%# locals: (comment: nil) %>`), `Model#attribute_or_method?` is always true (a mistyped attribute name renders as an empty string), `spin run db migrate` needs `--` (`spin run db -- migrate`), and under `SPINEL_GC_STRESS=1` the first `form_with` returns an empty string (suspected routing problem on the Spinel side).
- 2026-09-26: The application entry point was unified into `bin/<name>.rb` (`Cybertrain::Main`: server / migrate / db). `spin run gen` always writes `gen/views.rb`, and with `--embed-views` embeds `app/views/` as a string table. Production renders only from the embedded table (`Template::Engine.embedded`) and refuses to start if it is empty. Added `cybertrain migration` / `db` / `server` / `build`; `build` assembles `dist/` (the binary, public/, and storage/ and tmp/ if they do not exist). `bin/db.rb` is kept for migrations during development (`bin/<name>.rb` reads `gen/app`, so it cannot compile until the first migration creates `gen/models/`; `cybertrain migration` uses `spin run db -- migrate`, and production uses `dist/<name> migrate`). Cross builds and embedding public/ are out of scope.
- 2026-09-27 (up to PR #5): The CLI is also distributed as a gem (`gem install cybertrain`; `cybertrain new` writes the same tag as the dependency; D11). `cybertrain build` holds `tmp/cybertrain-build.lock` (containing the PID) until dist/ is assembled, and swaps in `dist/<name>` and `dist/public/` with rename (`.public.old`). The development server skips rebuilds while the lock is held and logs that it did, and removes and ignores locks with a dead PID or older than 30 minutes. When the port cannot be bound, it prints `PortInUse` (in use / permission denied) to stdout and exits with code 1. `schema:dump` writes only where `db/` exists (exit code 1 otherwise). Added `script/regen-snapshot`. `spin test` has 64 programs.
- 2026-10-22: File uploads (D16). On the framework side: `Multipart` / `MultipartForm` / `UploadedFile` / `BlobStore`, `Params#upload`, `Request#multipart_form`, `Controller#send_blob`, `form_with(multipart: true)` / `f.file_field`, and 3 `Config` keys. On the CLI side: `cybertrain generate attachment` and scaffold's `FIELD:attachment`. The gem E2E in CI generates and compiles a scaffold with an attachment, runs the generated tests, and checks upload and download with curl (byte-identical content, headers).
- 2026-10-22: Authentication (D15). `cybertrain generate authentication` writes the users / sessions migrations, the `User` / `Session` models, `SessionsController` / `RegistrationsController`, views, an insertion into `ApplicationController`, routes, and `test/authentication.rb`. On the framework side: `Cybertrain::Password` (PBKDF2-HMAC-SHA256), `Controller.skip_before_action`, `Mapper#resource`, and a rule that leaves the derived names of custom routes unnamed when they collide. The gem E2E in CI compiles the generated application and runs sign-up / sign-in integration tests.
- 2026-10-23: `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff` and `X-Frame-Options: SAMEORIGIN` became the default on every response (D14, D16). `Response#to_http` emits them only when no header of the same name is present. Added webp / avif / woff / ttf / otf / wasm and others to `Static::EXTENSIONS`. No `Config` switch was added; override with `set_header`.
- 2026-10-25: Application-defined settings (D17, issue #69). They are declared with `Config#setting_string` / `setting_int` / `setting_bool` and read with `string_setting` / `int_setting` / `bool_setting`. `help` prints the list of declarations, and the startup banner prints the current values (secrets masked). The regression test is `test/config_settings.rb`.
- 2026-10-30: An idle keep-alive connection waits for its next request in one park instead of `ACCEPT_POLL` slices, and the drain ends idle connections on shutdown by shutting their read side (D14). On one CPU, 1,000 idle connections cost 8.6% of it before (docs/performance-next.md 8). The Dockerfile `cybertrain new` writes, and examples/blog's, set `SPINEL_GC_FULL_INTERVAL=8` (docs/spinel-feedback.md 5).
- 2026-11-05: Phase 1 of the Rails-gap programme (D1; [the design](superpowers/specs/2026-11-05-phase1-realtime-views-processes-design.md) and [the plan](superpowers/plans/2026-11-05-phase1-realtime-views-processes-plan.md)), in five packages merged together. Streaming responses and the stream endpoint over Server-Sent Events and WebSocket (D36); the `cybertrain_messages` pub/sub, its poller and Turbo Stream broadcasts (D37); `case`, blocks and the `tag` builder in templates, application helpers, view components and rendering outside a request (D9, D38); job threads, heartbeats, fenced completion and per-kind concurrency (D28); the `worker` and `feeds` roles and the scheduler lease (D33, D39); BLOB columns (D40); the console and dbconsole (D41). New test programs: `test/stream.rb`, `stream_endpoint.rb`, `websocket_server.rb`, `response_stream.rb`, `sse.rb`, `turbo.rb`, `views_detached.rb`, `gen_helpers.rb`, `app_helpers.rb`, `gen_components.rb`, `component_render.rb`, `html_tag.rb`, `template_case.rb`, `template_blocks.rb`, `template_tag.rb`, `job_threads.rb`, `lease.rb`, `main_roles.rb`, `db_blob.rb`, `console.rb`, `gen_console.rb`, `main_console.rb`, `cli_generate_helper.rb`, `cli_generate_component.rb`; `test/jobs.rb` grew. `spin test` has 130 programs. spikes/NOTES.md gained rules 81 to 83 and 85: a relative constant path in a `rescue` clause can be silently not found (81); a lambda literal in a module-body ivar never learns its call sites' argument types (82); `gets` does not flush a partial STDOUT line first (83); `Queue#pop(timeout:)` works as under CRuby, so the stream hub waits with it (85).
- 2026-11-07: Phase 2 of the Rails-gap programme, the data layer (D1; [the design](superpowers/specs/2026-11-06-phase2-data-layer-design.md) and [the plan](superpowers/plans/2026-11-06-phase2-data-layer-plan.md), section 18 of the design for what differed). The decimal, json, date and bigint column types, Rails' datetime storage format and `db normalize`, and `Time.iso8601` / `rfc2822` / `strptime` (D42); the `where` forms, `where.not`, `or`, Integer Ranges and `where_between`, the class delegations, `scope` and the small Relation methods (D43); multi-column `pluck` and `pick` (D44); typed aggregates and `having_sql` (D45); `insert_all`, `insert_all!` and `upsert_all` (D46); declared associations, `has_many` as a Relation, `dependent:`, counter caches and `includes` (D47); the validators, `validate :method` and `if:` / `unless:` (D48); `encrypts` and `Cybertrain::Encryptor` (D49); `on_delete:`, partial and expression indexes, the rebuilding migrations, `reversible`, `db schema` and the Rails takeover (D50). examples/blog declares its associations, preloads the index's comments, validates title uniqueness, filters by date and has a `recent` scope. New test programs: `test/cast_types.rb`, `time_ext.rb`, `db_normalize.rb`, `relation_where.rb`, `pluck.rb`, `aggregates.rb`, `bulk_writes.rb`, `associations.rb`, `includes.rb`, `validators.rb`, `encryptor.rb`, `boot_encryption.rb`, `migration_rebuild.rb`, `schema_dumper_rails.rb`; `test/gen_models.rb` grew (scopes among them). `spin test` has 147 programs, and the blog's two programs run 30 tests. spikes/NOTES.md gained rules 86 to 88: a Range carries Integer ends only (86); class methods added to `Time` from Ruby source compile and dispatch (87); a stored block called with a base-class receiver runs the base class's stub, not the override (88).
- 2026-11-09: Phase 3 of the Rails-gap programme, jobs, delivery and outbound (D1; [the design](superpowers/specs/2026-11-07-phase3-jobs-outbound-design.md) and [the plan](superpowers/plans/2026-11-09-phase3-jobs-outbound-plan.md), section 18 of the design for what differed). Job classes in `app/jobs` with `retry_on`, `discard_on`, `retries_exhausted`, `limits_concurrency` with a key, the `discarded` state, `cybertrain generate job` and the job test helpers (D51); `Scheduler.cron` with time zones and the timetable in `cybertrain_schedules` (D52); the jobs page from `jobs_page` in the routes and `./<app> jobs` (D53); `Cybertrain::Cache` (D54); the HTTP client's verbs, streamed bodies, `get_to_file`, redirects, gzip and the Server-Sent Events parser (D55); `on_tick`, the feed supervisor, the exit of a `feeds` process after repeated deaths, `cybertrain_feeds`, `feeds status` and `503 feed stale` (D56); HTML mail and `deliver_later` (D57). `cybertrain new` writes `app/jobs/.keep`, a commented `jobs_page` line in `config/routes.rb`, a commented `cron` example in `config/app.rb`, `restart: unless-stopped` for the worker role and the feed-health and scheduler-zone variables in `.env.example`. examples/blog gains `NotifyCommentJob` (enqueued when a comment is posted), a `cron` task that prunes done jobs, the jobs page behind `BLOG_ADMIN_TOKEN` (a header, or a session set at `/admin/login`) and an article count cached for a minute. New test programs: `test/cron.rb`, `scheduler_cron.rb`, `gen_jobs.rb`, `gen_jobs_compiles.rb`, `cli_generate_job.rb`, `jobs_page.rb`, `main_jobs.rb`, `cache.rb`, `http_client_gzip.rb`, `sse_parser.rb`, `feeds_exit.rb`, `mailer_job.rb`, `job_helpers.rb`; `test/jobs.rb`, `job_threads.rb`, `scheduler.rb`, `http_client.rb`, `mailer.rb`, `websocket_subscription.rb`, `background.rb`, `health.rb`, `main_roles.rb`, `config_settings.rb`, `gen_routes.rb` and `gen_controllers.rb` grew. `spin test` has 161 programs. spikes/NOTES.md gained rules 89 to 91: a `rescue` arm naming a qualified class the program does not define matches by its last segment or swallows everything (89); a `yield` passed straight as an argument can run its block twice (90); `exit` on a thread other than the main one ends only that thread (91).
- 2026-11-11: Phase 4a of the Rails-gap programme, the web layer (D1; [the design](superpowers/specs/2026-11-10-phase4-web-layer-design.md) and [the plan](superpowers/plans/2026-11-10-phase4a-web-layer-plan.md), section 18 of the design for what differed). `namespace`, `scope`, resource blocks, `controller:`, `param:`, redirect routes and query strings, `format:` and `anchor:` in the path helpers (D58); the optional format extension, `request.format`, `respond_to` and implicit rendering by format (D59); controller and model concerns expanded by gen, and `helper_method` (D60); `params.expect` and nested `permit`, `send_data`, `fresh_when` / `stale?` / `expires_in`, every status symbol, the `rate_limit` options and the shared count in the cache, the AES-256-GCM session and the cookie jar with openssl in every program, the Content Security Policy with nonces, `Permissions-Policy`, `force_ssl` and `CYBERTRAIN_HOSTS` (D61); `Cybertrain::NumberHelper`, the form builder's `select` family, radio buttons and input types and the standalone form tags, per-controller layouts, Turbo frame and stream helpers, `time_tag`, `current_page?` and partial and component collections (D62); digested assets, CSS `url()` rewriting, the import map and their helpers and immutable serving (D63). `cybertrain new` writes `app/assets/stylesheets/application.css` (no longer `public/style.css`), `app/javascript/application.js`, `config/importmap.rb`, the layout's asset and CSP tags, the commented CSP block, `/public/assets/` in `.gitignore`, OpenSSL in the Dockerfile and `asset_path` in `config/deploy.yml`. examples/blog serves its digested stylesheet and a logo through `url()`, loads Turbo through the import map, answers `/articles.json` and `/articles/:id.json` through `respond_to` and a posted comment with Turbo Stream, lists and deletes articles under `/admin/articles` behind the `AdminGate` concern with the jobs page beside them, formats counts and dates with `number_with_delimiter` and `time_tag`, orders the index with a `select_tag`, and sends a Content Security Policy with nonces. New test programs: `test/assets.rb`, `collections.rb`, `controller_formats.rb`, `cookie_jar.rb`, `csp.rb`, `force_ssl.rb`, `form_tags.rb`, `gen_assets.rb`, `gen_concerns.rb`, `gen_model_concerns.rb`, `helper_method.rb`, `http_caching.rb`, `number_helper.rb`, `rate_limit_store.rb`, `send_data.rb`, `turbo_helpers.rb`; `test/gen_routes.rb`, `gen_routes_compiles.rb`, `gen_namespaced.rb`, `router.rb`, `http_request.rb`, `http_response.rb`, `helpers.rb`, `gen_controllers.rb`, `controller_views.rb`, `controller_status.rb`, `params.rb`, `session.rb`, `openssl_dispatch.rb`, `rate_limit.rb`, `host_authorization.rb`, `config_settings.rb`, `form_builder.rb`, `component_render.rb`, `gen_components.rb`, `static.rb`, `dev_watcher.rb` and `cli_new.rb` grew. `spin test` has 177 programs, and the blog's two programs run 53 tests. spikes/NOTES.md gained rules 92 to 98: a never-run block is compiled against the code around it and its bare calls refused (92); a `def` inside a never-run block is defined anyway (93); type inference that did not converge in the blog's generator (94); `binding.local_variable_get` reads a keyword parameter named after a keyword (95); `strftime` with a built pattern on a Time-or-Date value returns the pattern (96); an ivar String passed to a stored lambda is refused (97); spinel's own C output left in a package tree is not compiled (98).
